- **No eyebrow on mobile.** Also tried and rejected — with 16px body copy, real image sizes and a normal gap there isn't room, and it reads wordy. The logo carries the venue name; the headline carries the daypart. (Desktop keeps the eyebrow.)
- **NOW marker** — a pip on whichever tile is *now*, **independent of what's selected**. It sits on an inactive tile whenever the guest has moved. Selection and now are two axes, not one; the slider has the same split (knob vs now-marker).
- **Active tile marking** — a 2px **Peppercorn** keyline (decided 17 September 2026 — as built and as the component draws it; this bullet had said `--accent-2`), plus the size difference. **Inactive tiles are dimmed** rather than the active one being brightened: same unlit → lit logic as outline → fill, no new device, and only one photo is ever at full strength, so an uneven photo set doesn't show four at once. *Current build uses an outline plus a glow; the glow is a selection halo that appears nowhere else in the system — restyle pending, not blocking.*
- **Layering stays a desktop device.** The offset Lemon/Chili sheets from the menu-page hero need roughly 14px of offset to read as stacked paper; at mobile tile sizes they shrink to ~4px and read as colour fringing, closer to a print misregistration than to depth. Mobile uses keyline + size only.

### Tile states

| | is now | not now |
|---|---|---|
| **selected** | large · keyline · NOW pip | large · keyline · "Back to now" appears |
| **not selected** | small · dimmed · NOW pip | small · dimmed |

### Behaviour

- The tapped tile **moves** into the large slot — never a crossfade. If the photo the guest touched dissolves, they lose track of what they just did.
- **No auto-revert.** An explicit choice is not overruled by the clock — "time proposes, memory disposes," same principle as the language switch. "Back to now" is the only way back, which argues for giving it a fixed home rather than a chip that appears and disappears in the hero.
- `prefers-reduced-motion`: instant swap, no theme cross-dissolve.
- **This is now the mobile day/night control** — tapping a dinner or party tile darkens the site. It therefore replaces the peek on mobile entirely, and feeds the open question in The day/night concept → Under consideration: the mobile hero now does what the peek tab was for.

### Colour roles in the hero

- ~~**Headline and the active tile's keyline** take `--accent-2` — Olive by day, Lemon or Lime by night.~~ **Superseded (author, 17 September 2026): the headline is Chili in both modes at every width, as on desktop.** The "red flooding the frame" concern below was weighed and the desktop composition kept; if red does flood on a phone with red-heavy photography, the answer is the photo set, not the headline. The keyline is Peppercorn at every width — decided the same day (author), see Anatomy.
- 🔶 **Red floods the night hero on phones — reopened by the author, 20 Sep 2026.** Wordmark, headline and Reserve are all Chili, on top of a Chili sheet and the red pot. The author tried a Lemon and a Lime headline and a Lime button on the device and kept none: the Chili button stays (it matches the desktop Reserve tab and reads right as the main CTA). Untried until now: **Cream**, which `design.md` §2.3 names as night's highlight *text* ("text in Parchment (body) and Cream (highlight) · Chili leads") — Lemon and Lime are the loud highlight hues, Cream is the soft one, and it does not collide with the Lemon NOW pip the way a Lemon headline did. Options on the live page: `Claude outputs/night-red-contact-sheet.png` — A Cream · B Parchment · C Paprika · D outlined (rule-breaker: outline means *not current*) · E Cream + no Chili in the lit tile's sheets. Recommendation on record: **A, night only, phones and the portrait bento only**; day and desktop keep the Chili headline. **Built 20 Sep 2026 as A — the author was between Lemon and Cream; Cream is in, and it is one token to change.** The headline takes `--text-highlight`, which the theme already defined and nothing used: Chili by day, Cream by night. The case against Lemon: it is already the colour of the Now pill, the button's label and bell and the hamburger, so a Lemon headline makes "highlight only" the dominant colour of the screen, puts the largest type and the smallest state marker in one colour (the rule two bullets down — the pip would have to move to Lime) and sets two loud hues, Lemon over Chili, against each other at the CTA. The author's two worries about Cream, checked: *one more colour* — true of this screen, not of the system: same hue as Lemon (55° against 56°), only lighter, and it splits roles cleanly — Cream is text, Lemon is signals; *not intense enough* — it is paler, not weaker: **16.5:1 on Peppercorn against Lemon's 15.3:1**. To switch: `color: var(--lemon)` in `hero.css` → bento `.hero-headline`, and re-open the pip. **On trial since later the same day (author): Lemon headline + Parchment subhead at night, phones and the portrait bento — the author tests it on the device and comes back with the final call; Cream is the fallback.** The subhead moves from Mushroom (muted) to Parchment, night's body colour (`design.md` §2.3), which also lifts it to the desktop's subhead colour. If Lemon stays, two things follow and are *not done yet*: the Now pill shares the headline's colour (the rule two bullets down — move the pip to Lime at night, or retire the rule), and the button's Lemon label sits under a Lemon headline.
- 🔶 **PROTOTYPE — the "now" word, behind `?hl=1` (author's idea, 21 Sep 2026; Figma 2482:70566, desktop try 1680:71623).** One word of the hero headline takes the louder colour of a pair. **The rule that makes it a system, not decoration: it is the word the clock chose** — MORNING · PUMPKIN SOUP · TONIGHT · COCKTAIL — and **the closed-hours headlines carry none** (nothing is on, nothing lit). The frames pick differently per headline (COCKTAIL the subject, SWEET DREAMS the greeting); re-sync them if the rule stays. Contact sheet of every pair: `Claude outputs/headline-pairs-contact-sheet.png`.
  - **Night, ≤ 991 — Cream / Lemon (author's pick to try, from the tablet breakpoint down — every tablet, not only the portrait bento).** Settles the Lemon-vs-Cream trial above with both, and turns *the NOW pip never takes the headline's colour* (below) from a clash into an echo — Lemon = now, twice; the exception must be written there if this ships. Cream against Lemon is 1.08:1 in luminance: the split is saturation only, and it reads.
  - **Day, ≤ 991 — `&hlday=`**: `green` Olive solid (3.68:1 on Parchment) · `red` Chili / Paprika word · `red2` Paprika / Chili word · `red3` Deep Chili / Chili word. Read on the sheet: the two reds are 1.37:1 apart and look like a print fade, not a highlight, whichever way round; Paprika is 2.51:1 on Parchment, so `red` puts the *lit* word under 3:1 and `red2` the rest of the line; `red3` clears contrast but the dark word reads as the emphasis, which is the wrong word. And any red pair keeps the phone's red stack (wordmark, headline, Reserve). **No day pair lights the way Lemon does at night.** ~~`green2`, Olive + Dark Olive~~ — built, read as black ink, removed with the colour (below).
  - **Desktop, ≥ 992 — `&hld=`**: `a` Chili / Paprika word, both modes (the Figma try) · `b` **the word is Chili in both modes**, on a Paprika line · `c` as b but everything over 3:1 (day Deep Chili / Chili, night Paprika / Chili) · `d` day as c, night Cream / Lemon. **Finding (author, on the first sheet): in a red pair the eye takes Chili as the highlight on either ground.** The first build lit COCKTAIL in Paprika by night ("the brighter colour is the louder one on dark", 6.1 vs 4.5:1) and the author read it as the *unlit* word — saturation beats luminance here. So the lit word is Chili everywhere in b and c; `a` keeps the other reading for comparison. Costs unchanged: by day a Paprika line is 2.51:1 on Parchment (b), and the two reds are 1.37:1 apart.
  - Mechanics: `daypart-engine.js` → `markNowWord()` wraps the word after the scramble resolves (the ride writes plain text, so the word lights as the last beat); `white-space: nowrap` keeps PUMPKIN SOUP whole; `hero.css` → PROTOTYPE, one pair = two direct rules. Without `?hl=1` nothing changes. If promoted: the split moves into the copy per language (RU word order), and the flag code goes.
- **Reserve is Chili in both modes.** One CTA colour across modes beats one optimised per mode: Lemon and Lime both fail as buttons on Parchment (too little separation from the ground), and a Peppercorn primary in day mode was rejected. Red therefore leaves the headline, which is also the fix for red flooding the frame — logo, button and headline were all Chili at once on top of red-heavy food photography.
- **The NOW pip takes the remaining accent, never the headline's.** A Lemon headline with a Lemon pip puts the largest type and the smallest state marker in the same colour, in the same viewport.

### CTAs

One button (Reserve) plus a **named scroll cue** — "What's cooking tonight ↓" — instead of a second menu button. The hero already shows four photographs of food, so appetite is served above the fold; the cue carries the menu path, which is why it must name its destination. "Scroll down for more" promises nothing and doesn't do that job.

**Condition attached:** the section below (Menu highlights) has to *open* with food. If it opens with a Molot headline and the photos sit another 200px down, a guest who scrolls once sees no menu content and the hero's missing menu CTA costs a visit.

### Cost to plan for

Four dayparts × four tiles is **16 art-directed photos** rather than the slider's four, maintained by the team in WordPress. Decide which tiles can be shared across dayparts before shooting. A bento is also only as good as its worst photo and shows several at once — the dimming rule above is partly insurance against that.

### Not carried over to mobile

- **The eyebrow** and **the second (menu) CTA** — both present on desktop, both cut here for space. See Desktop — home hero.
- **The giant Molot wordmark in the hero.** Its job is a section seam, not an introduction (Layout notes); asking it to name the venue above the fold is what made it look clipped by the browser chrome. It stays lower on the page, where its size is an asset rather than a fold problem.

### Build status (Sep 2026) — where the build and the rules above differ

Built from `home-hero-bento-stack` 2048:126962 (night) / 2048:127658 (day) and the `meal-home-container-opt-2` component 1659:35997. Decisions taken in the build session, and the rules they touch:

- **Grid-first, decided.** Tiles → headline → copy → Reserve, then the connector. Nav (76, fixed) + hero = one viewport; the content block is **centred** between nav and connector (author's call after seeing it in the browser; bottom-anchoring was tried first), never less than 24px under the nav, 36px above the connector.
- **The colour stack is on mobile — trial, against "Layering stays a desktop device".** The author chose to try the stacked cards first for consistency with the desktop hero. At the mobile size (234×156 photo, 242 container, 8px right/bottom padding) the offsets are the desktop ones, not the ~4px the rule above feared. If it stays, rewrite that bullet; if it goes, the keyline-only variant is one CSS block. The stack follows the **desktop per-daypart recipes and alternating tilt** (Desktop — home hero), not the component's single Lime/Lemon/Chili order and −1° for every daypart; the Now pill rides the tilted photo as on desktop, where the component draws it level.
- ~~**Keyline is Peppercorn, not `--accent-2`.**~~ **Decided (author, 17 September 2026): Peppercorn, as built.** The component's active photo carries a 2px Peppercorn border, the same as desktop; the Anatomy rule that said `--accent-2` is corrected above. Nothing to change in the build.
- ~~**Headline colour is Olive by day, Lemon by night — and the NOW pip is Lemon too.**~~ **Resolved (author, 17 September 2026): Chili at every width, the desktop rule.** The night clash of a Lemon headline beside the Lemon pip goes with it. The frames draw Olive / Lemon — re-sync them. 🔶 **Back on the table, 20 Sep 2026:** a Lemon night headline is on trial on the bento, so the Lemon-beside-Lemon-pip clash is live again until the author's final call (Colour roles, above) — if Lemon stays, the pip moves to Lime at night or this rule is retired.
- **Headline size is a raw 32px** (Molot, 104%, 4%) in the final frame, not `Size/HomeHero` → `HeadlineFontSize` (56, Party 52). Author is keeping 32 pending notes; `design.md` §3.3 carries the same note.
- **No scroll cue in the final frame.** The frame runs Reserve only, with the "AT SWEET PEPPER" connector below it; the `opt3-link` variant (a tappable connector) was tried and dropped, `opt3-word` won. The CTA rule below (named cue + Highlights must open with food) is therefore not met yet — the scroll-cue hero variant is parked, and the Highlights condition is still owed when that section is built.
- **Tile move is a real move, on the house spring.** A tap FLIPs every tile from its old box to its new one over 800ms Gentle (position by transform; width and height animated — see the Motion language exception); dim overlay, sheets, keyline and the day/night ground cross-fade on the flat curve. Reduced motion: instant, no cross-fade. The glow is gone — dimming + keyline + size only, as the rule asks.
- **Not built:** "Back to now" (no way home after a tap, same gap as desktop), the language nudge (hidden on mobile — it has no mobile design), the eyebrow and the second CTA (cut, as specified).
- **Fits:** 402×874, 390×844, 430×932. A 375×667 phone overruns the viewport by 4px (content height).
- **The bento reaches portrait tablets (Sep 2026).** The phone block's query is now phones *or* 768–991 at `min-height: 1000px`, with a tablet block for what differs — see Grid → Tablet → *The home hero in the band*. The FLIP in `daypart-engine.js` gates on the same query.

### Open (mobile hero)

- ✓ **Closed state** — built 20 Sep 2026; one engine, so it landed here with the desktop row. See *Open (desktop hero)* → Closed state.
- ~~**Text-first or grid-first**~~ — resolved grid-first (Sep 2026, see Build status).
- ~~Active-tile treatment — see the glow note above.~~ — the glow is gone, the keyline is Peppercorn (decided 17 Sep 2026) and the night Lemon headline / Lemon pip clash went with the Chili headline the same day. Nothing open here.
- Whether the colour stack stays on mobile (trial, see Build status). If it does, rewrite "Layering stays a desktop device".

## Mobile — Highlights (built Sep 2026)

From `HighlightsSection` 2048:126987, as drawn. 48px rhythm throughout (desktop 80): connector → title block → card rail → the two menu CTAs, full width and stacked → connector. Cards keep their 320 width and become a horizontal snap rail that bleeds across the 16px gutters; the cropped third card is the scroll affordance — no dots, no scrollbar, same call as the About quote rail. Connectors stay the desktop SVGs scaled to 370 (Section connectors → Width matching); the Figma `SectionLinkMobile` treatment is still not adopted.

**Section title on phones — component-wide.** The rules from the Figma mobile section header (`sectionTitle-Mobile` 1164:51509, used on every mobile screen) went into the shared section-header component, so every section that uses it inherits them: title block full width, CTAs under it at full width, eyebrow colours unchanged from desktop (Olive by day, Cream by night — the frame's Avocado was a stale instance, decided Sep 2026). **Eyebrow size — decided Sep 2026:** the Figma component sets it in `Heading/Captions` (Molot 16); the build keeps **H3 18 at every width**. Tested in the browser, 18 works on a phone, it stays consistent with desktop, and it avoids adding the second small-Molot exception `design.md` §3.3 leaves open. Revisit only if 18 starts crowding a real title; the Figma component is the one to re-sync then.

**Home → menu links — the whole set (Sep 2026).** Recorded here because the convention was settled doing the highlight cards, and it governs every link from the home page into the menu.

**Only one menu state renders per request**, so the state travels with the anchor: `#infusions` does not exist in the food state — verified against the rendered page, not assumed. Food is the default, so food anchors need no query. Every destination below was checked against the live markup.

| from | destination |
|---|---|
| Card — House infusions | `/menu/bar/#infusions` |
| Card — Grilled wings | `/menu/#bar-snacks` |
| Card — Pot roast | `/menu/#hot-dishes` |
| Highlights header — Drinks menu | `/menu/bar/` |
| Highlights header — Food menu | `/menu/` |
| Bar preview — Explore the drinks menu | `/menu/bar/` |
| Kitchen preview — Explore the food menu | `/menu/` |
| Hero daypart button | per daypart — Desktop — home hero → Composition |

**All eight were dead before this pass.** The four section/preview CTAs pointed at `#bar-menu` and `#kitchen-menu`, the hero at `#menu` — none of those ids exists anywhere on the home page; the cards had no link at all. After the pass the only same-page anchors left on the home page are `#page` and `#primary`, and both resolve.

**The card is a door.** A highlight card renders as an `<a>` when it has a `url`, and as a `<div>` when it doesn't, so the component stays usable display-only. It was a `<div>` carrying `cursor: pointer` until now — it looked clickable, was not, and no keyboard could reach it. Focus gets a Chili outline by day, Lime by night.

**Card rows align by structure, not by centring (fixed Sep 2026).** `.highlight-card` was `justify-content: center`, which hung the whole stack in the middle of the equal-height box, so any difference in content height carried the photo with it — measured at 900: photos starting at dy **26 / 15 / 0**, tags at **361 / 373 / 388**. Now `flex-start` + `align-items: stretch`, the tag pinned with `margin-top: auto`, and the description clamped to three lines. Photo, title and tag share a baseline on all three cards at every width. Worth recording because it was misread as a photo-size problem: the photos were never different heights — `aspect-ratio: 3/2` on equal-width cards cannot differ — only differently placed.

**Open:**

- 🔶 **Card row alignment — the copy fix is a stopgap, settle it at the final review.** "Pepper's pot roast" was shortened to **"Pot roast"** (author, Sep 2026) so the title holds one line. That hides the symptom rather than fixing it — and moves it. The title row is the unstable part, and the **price** wraps to its own row independently of the title, on a different number of cards at every width. Measured header heights (Infusions / Wings / Pot roast):

| viewport | 768 | 900 | 1024 | 1152 | 1280 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| header height | **90 / 60 / 32** | 60 / 60 / 32 | 60 / 32 / 32 | 60 / 32 / 32 | 32 / 32 / 32 |

The three cards agree **only at the 1280 design width**. Shortening the title made "Pot roast" the one card whose price never wraps, so it became the odd one out at 768–1152 instead of the tall one — the drift changed shape rather than going away, and at 768 the spread is now 58px. Photo, title and tag stay aligned throughout; it is the description that moves. Two structural fixes were built and measured. **Subgrid** (`grid-template-rows: subgrid` on the card, `grid-row: span 4`) aligns all four rows at every width, truncates nothing, leaves no dead space on rows where every title is short, and shrinks the card ~16px; verified down to the 320px phone rail with snap intact. A fixed two-line title reserve does **not** work — it still left 29px of drift at 1152, because the *price* wraps independently of the title. Subgrid needs one decision first: `.highlight-card-header` is `flex-wrap: wrap`, so the price sometimes takes its own row and sometimes doesn't; Figma specifies title and price on **one** row, and the build has diverged at every width below ~1440 since that wrap was added. Either make the stack deliberate (`flex-direction: column`) or keep the Figma row and shorten the price copy — "From 150 ₽" is the one that forces it. **Truncating the title to one line was considered and rejected:** the dish name is the product, an ellipsis in Molot at 4% tracking reads as a rendering fault rather than a choice, and RU runs longer than EN so it would fire *more* often after Polylang, not less.
- **Section description still says "Pepper's pot roast"** while the card now says "Pot roast". Prose and label can differ, but decide it rather than leaving it as a leftover.
- **Description copy.** The mobile mockup runs only the second sentence ("One of the only places in Yaroslavl…"); the build renders the desktop two-sentence version at both widths. Decide whether mobile gets the short cut, and if so whether that is a copy rule (shorter teasers on phones) or a one-off.
- **"Highlights must open with food"** (Desktop — home hero → CTA rule) is still owed: on a phone the first card lands ~380px below the section top, under the connector, the title and six lines of description.
- **Card rail on tablets (768–991px)** — answered by the Sep 2026 tablet pass (Grid → Tablet): the desktop three-up grid holds, and on the ramped gutter the cards run **229–285px**. Still in no mockup; the widths were settled in the browser, not in Figma.

And the bar CTA's pepper glyph was invisible on phones: Figma exports share `clipPath` ids across files, `Pepper.svg` (the button) and `c-Pepper.svg` (the drawer's "you are here" marker) both define `clip0_52_3199`, and on phones the drawer's copy comes first in the document. Inline SVGs now go through `sweet_pepper_inline_svg()` (`inc/inline-svg.php`), which suffixes every id per instance; the button component and the drawer marker use it, the other `file_get_contents` call sites still don't — move them over as they're touched.

## Mobile — Contacts (built Sep 2026)

From `Contacts` 1198:53132, as drawn. **The form is gone on phones** (author's call: it made the section too long); the desktop keeps it until said otherwise. In its place, top to bottom at 24px: the title block with a **phone-only short description** ("Walk-ins always welcome…", Body 16 since 18 Sep 2026; was Body/Secondary 14, retired — the section-header component now takes a `description_mobile` that replaces the desktop paragraph ≤ 767px) → the reserve drawer's **booking block** in the flow (phone CTA on the bar-state engine, then VK / Instagram half-width, then "Usually answer in 20 minutes") → a **"Get directions" band** bleeding across the gutters (map **360–400px by viewport** — `clamp(360px, 100dvh − 352px, 400px)`, so the band fits one screen under the header on any phone and "Looking for more?" fits with it from ~750px tall; the frame's 240 read too short in a browser — plus "Copy Address" and "Yandex Maps ↗" chips in the Interaction rule's chip style, Golos Medium 13) → **"Looking for more?"** with a Visit-page link. The desktop's social CTAs, address bar and form are hidden on phones; the three phone blocks are hidden on desktop. Bar state now applies to every phone CTA on the page, not only the drawer's.

**Address bar wraps instead of breaking (Sep 2026) — a desktop-side fix.** `.contacts-map__bar` takes `flex-wrap: wrap` and a 12px gap at the base, so the chips drop to a second row only when they and the address genuinely stop fitting. It was curing a real overflow: between **992 and 1095** the contacts split leaves the map card just 429–459px against a row needing 434 plus padding, so at 1000 the address was clipped by the Copy chip and Directions ran **37px past the card edge**. Wrapping derives the threshold from the content rather than adding another hand-picked breakpoint — worth preferring wherever a fixed component width meets a fluid column. With the gap counted the wrap now fires across **995–1130**, slightly wider than the overflow it cures, and the bar is one row again from 1135. Re-scanned 985–1220 in 5px steps: no overflow at any width.

**Open:**

- **Striping — settled (Sep 2026):** the build keeps the desktop alternation (Events Soft Peppercorn, Contacts Peppercorn) with the directions band on the *other* ground (Paper by day, Soft Peppercorn by night); the mobile frames (About and Events both Peppercorn, Contacts Soft Peppercorn) are the ones to re-sync.
- **Map provider.** Phones show the same Google My Maps embed as desktop; the Visit page geo-detects Yandex for RU. The "Yandex Maps" chip links out regardless. Decide whether the home map should geo-detect too (one class on the container).
- **Chip size — settled (Sep 2026):** Golos Medium 13 (the Interaction rule's chip style) in the frame's 100px pill with 8/16 padding; the frame's 14px was an undocumented paragraph style and 16 would be too heavy on a phone chip. Re-sync the frame.
- **Day colours** are derived (no day frame): sub-headings Olive, lead Soft Peppercorn, link Olive.

The set lives in `assets/sectionLinks/about/` as **desktop-width exports (~1080 wide) of the Figma `SectionLink` component**, colours baked per ground — About is a fixed composition, so one file per word/state serves day and night (the bar-menu precedent). **Both breakpoints, decided (author, Sep 2026):** the same nine seams on desktop, 80px rhythm, so About matches home and menu. *Why the exports were redone:* the first set was the `SectionLinkMobile` component at 370 wide, and it read too bright on phones — same colours as home, but 42px Molot with a 1px outside stroke where the home files are 122px Molot with the same 1px stroke scaled down to a third; measured, the phone set carried ~2.3× the ink of a home connector at 370. Desktop exports scaled down restore parity (ink within a few percent of home at both grounds). Reflection opacity follows **the ground it opens**, not `data-theme` (About never themes): 50% over a light section, 30% over a dark one on desktop, and full over a dark one on phones — the home rules, keyed to the section instead of the hour. *Re-measured in the browser (Sep 2026), all seventeen connectors at 1440 and 402, day and night forced: every reflection reads 50 over Parchment/Paper and 30 over Peppercorn/Soft Peppercorn on desktop, 100 over dark on phones; every foot word 100; night changes nothing on About. The home page under the same probe: 50 / 30 by hour on desktop, 100 by night on phones — the same numbers, keyed to the clock. The phone Figma frames draw `SectionLinkMobile` at 30 (reflection) and 50 with a 0.5px Mushroom rule (word) — the component the SVG rule did not adopt, so the build and the frames differ by design there.* The exports live in Figma on the Desktop page in two utility frames, *About connectors (desktop exports)* (instances, editable) and *About connectors (flattened for export)* (the text nodes the SVGs came from; delete and re-flatten after any edit). Rules touched: WORD OF MOUTH doubled as the connector and the eyebrow under it, so the eyebrow became **PEOPLE SPEAK** — a connector never repeats the eyebrow of the section it opens. Still owed: the nine judged as a set (Voice), the register-collapse check at phone width (a 370-wide connector sits near H1; the seam cut is what keeps it out of the state vocabulary), and RU twins.
- **Header:** the fixed Peppercorn bar (Top nav → Mobile header → About and Visit on phones), page ground Peppercorn under it.
- **Hero:** eyebrow → H1 (36) → lead (16), one 16px gap between the three (the lead's own paragraph margins are zeroed on phones — they were doubling the spacing; the frame draws 8 between eyebrow and title, 16 is the author's call) → 36 → a 2 + 3 grid of the five index cards (3:2 over 1:1, 12px gap — equal widths per row because the cards are peers with no state, unlike the home bento) → **48 to the connector**, as the other phone heroes (home: 36 + the connector's 12); bottom-anchored in nav + 100dvh, checked by the author at 375×667 and 430×932 (a 24px column gap would sit better on the SE — noted, not built); the desktop's 50%-dimmed rest state and dark pill become full-strength photo + Lemon caption label at rest, since touch has no hover to lift them. **Pressed state unified at every width** to the shared card recipe (Lime 1px + `inset 2px -2px 8px` at 25%, no radius change); the hero's own 24px inset and 4→8px radius jump are gone, the hover rules are gated behind `(hover: hover)`, and the inset shadow sits on the overlay because the photo covers the card's own. *Desktop still rests the cards dimmed — open: bring it to the phone rest state, since "dimmed" means inactive and none of the five is.*

- **How it feels — cloud over one quote (decided Sep 2026).** First pass dropped the cloud on phones (it and a three-card rail couldn't share a screen, so the one-clock coupling would have been invisible); the author brought it back with the rail cut to **one card**. The cloud is the row layout (the field's phone fallback) as **wrapping rows, centred, at a phone size trio of 40 / 26 / 18** — still three fixed sizes, hue = tier, outline → fill — about 300px tall for the 16 words; the viewport shows exactly one card, **sized by JS to the tallest quote** (the three-card viewport was cut to the first three cards and clipped the taller ones as the rail turned — the cropping the author saw). Cloud + card ≈ 500px, so the coupling stays in one view down to a 375×667 phone. **Touch grammar:** a tap fills the word, dims the others to 50% (30% until 21 Sep 2026) and swaps the quote; the heartbeat fills the next word and swaps the quote and un-dims; the 8 s seizure applies. ~~Nothing moves on phones (no centring).~~ *(Reversed 21 Sep 2026 — see the end of this entry.)* The card still swipes (scroll-snap), but the cropped-neighbour "many more" signal is gone — the cloud is the control now. Tier sizes come from the template as an inline `--cloud-size`; phones override through `--cloud-size-phone` (an inline custom property beats any selector). Cost kept in view: the `about-page-copy.md` desktop sizes (64 / 40 / 26 → 56 / 36 / 24 in the build) now have a third trio; one rule in the copy doc, not three. **Revised the same day:** the phone cloud is the field, not rows — the orbit was the thing the author wanted kept, so phones get the same placement and drift (details under Part 3 → Cloud layouts B and E → Shared); rows remain the no-JS state. And hue came off the tier at both widths (Part 3). **Two phone faults fixed 21 Sep 2026 (found on the test site).** *Slivers of the neighbouring cards:* the viewport is the tallest card + 16 and the neighbours sat a fixed 24px away, so any quote ~45px shorter than the tallest showed both of them at its edges. Every card now centres in a slot as tall as the viewport's inside (`--quote-slot`, set by `sizeViewport()` below 992, cleared above); measured with each of the 20 quotes centred at 402, 375 and 834: the nearest neighbour stays 24px outside. *Shaved words:* the phone field is crowded enough that the spiral's last resort (inset 0) is used — one word at 402, four at 375, five at 360 — and the ±3–6px orbit carried those past the field's `overflow: hidden`. Phones drop the clip (they have no click-to-centre, and the overshoot lands in the 16px gutter). **The chosen word centres on phones too, and the field has the room for it (author, 21 Sep 2026 — without the reshuffle the phone cloud "felt too rigid").** This reverses *Nothing moves on phones* above. What it took, all in `how-it-feels.js`, settled in a simulation of the file's own maths and then tap-tested on the page: (1) **the phone field's height follows the words** — area = 1.7 × the words' own area (each box + the 12px gap) ÷ width: **435 tall at 402, 449 at 390, 469 at 375, 490 at 360** (was a flat 380, where the words filled 67–76% of the field, the spiral fell back to gap 0 / inset 0 and, at 360, piled INCLUSIVE on WELCOMING at rest). Swept 288–398px: 1.5 piles at rest at 38 widths, 1.6 at 9, 1.7 at none. It also holds for RU, whose words will be longer. (2) **The commit tries the full move first, then shorter ones** — centre with a 12px cleared margin (desktop 28), then a 4px margin, then 60% and 30% of the way, then stay put; the first layout with no overlaps wins. About three taps in four centre fully. (3) Two solver faults that phones exposed, fixed at every width: words the spiral had rested at the field's edge were clamped 16px inward into their neighbours on every commit, and pairs rested closer than 12px were asked for 12 and driven into a wall. (4) While a word is chosen the others orbit at a third (phones only) — a packed field at full swing drifted neighbours into each other; and at rest a phone word never swings further than half-way to its nearest neighbour. *Measured: every one of the 16 words tapped at 402, 375 and 360 — no overlap over 4px; at rest over 12 s the worst overlap is 2px of box, no ink (was 5–24px).* **Inactive words dim to 50%, not 30% (author, same day), every width** — at 30 a 1px phone outline all but left the page, and fill-against-outline already names the chosen word. 🔶 **Cost, on the record:** the taller field breaks *the coupling stays in one view down to a 375×667 phone* — cloud + card now runs 652px of 798 visible at 402×874 and 665 of 768 at 390×844 (fine), but **701 of 591 at 375×667 and 722 of 664 at 360×740**: on small phones a tap swaps a quote that is partly below the fold. Levers if it matters: fewer words on phones, or the trio 40 / 26 / 18 a step smaller. **Revised the same evening — twelve words on phones (author: "I would try fewer words").** The field's cost is *wide* words, not many words — settled in the simulation (now `tools/how-it-feels-field-sim.mjs`): dropping the four small words saves nothing (they are the filler that tucks into gaps; the field still needs ~450px), dropping **the four widest mid-tier words — WONDERFUL, CHARMING, PLEASANT, MAGNETIC —** brings it to **380 / 410 / 429px at 402 / 375 / 360** (was 435 / 469 / 490 for sixteen), and dropping SOCIABLE and BELOVED as well (ten words) to 323 / 349 / 365. Built as a flag per word in `how-it-feels.php` (`'phone' => false`), so the list is the author's to edit; **a flagged word takes its quotes with it** (16 of 20 remain) — a quote never shows without its word. `how-it-feels.js` removes the flagged nodes once, at load, below 768; rows (no JS) keep all sixteen. `FIELD_AREA_PHONE` is **2.0**, tuned to this list (the 1.7 above was for sixteen), and a field that still cannot seat a word grows 20px and re-places, up to six times — a longer list or RU costs height, never a pile. *Measured on the page: 12 words / 16 quotes below 768, 16 / 20 above; every word tapped at 402, 375 and 360 — no overlaps, 26 of 36 taps centre fully; rest overlap 0.0px over 20 s.* **The float is back to its designed size:** with sixteen words the half-way-to-the-neighbour cap was biting (touching pairs swung 1.5px) — the author noticed the cloud had gone still; with twelve every word swings 7–12px peak to peak, the full phone orbit (desktop 14–24). After a tap the others stay at a third for the 8 s hold, by design. **One-screen check, updated:** 597 of 798 at 402×874, **661 of 664 at 360×740 (fits)**, 642 of 591 at 375×667 — the SE class still runs 51px over with twelve; ten words would fit it (581). *Open: the author's pick of which words stay; whether 667-tall phones matter enough for ten.* **Cloud → card gap 24 → 0 on phones (author, on trial, 21 Sep 2026).** The blank under the words was never just the gap: field inset 16 + spiral slack ~10 + gap 24 + viewport 4 + the card's centring in its tallest-quote slot 4–22 = **59–77px from the lowest word to the card** (tallest–shortest quote); without the gap **35–53**, against ~62 from the title to the highest word — the cloud sits nearer its card than its title, which is the relationship. The nearest a floating word comes to the tallest card's tilted corner is ~19px. The 18px swing between quotes stays (top-aligning the card would move it all under the card: card → CTA 44–81). One-screen check with it: 573 of 798 at 402×874, 637 of 664 at 360×740, 618 of 591 at 375×667. `.about-cloud`'s own `padding: 16px 0` does nothing once the field is placed (explicit border-box height, absolute words) — left as it is for the rows state. The active glow stays as drawn (both Figma sets carry it) although `about-page-copy.md` says "no glow, per the flat-effects rule" — conflict left to the author.

Axis, heat and ticks don't survive the switch — that closes the "does the heat line survive" question in The Pepper Story → Open for phones. **Ledger** keeps the ticket *edge* (scallops in the ground colour above, the card colour below), number left / label right, ruled rows. **Checked against frame 1490:78397 (Sep 2026):** the founder photo is square again (the `<img>` height attribute was pinning 436px once the column got narrower — `height: auto`, all widths); the year control is the frame's tray — Parchment tray, Paper segments with no rules between, Chili + Lemon for the current year, no card border; the ticket tilts 1° like the quote card, its header carries a Mushroom rule, rows are Molot 24 / Golos 13 (Caption; was 14 until 18 Sep 2026) on Avocado rules, bottom-aligned. **Count-up never fired on short phones:** the entrance watched the whole section at a 35% threshold, and a 2000px section never shows 35% of itself in a 667px viewport, so the counters sat at 0; it now keys off the timeline (half in view) and the clock off the ledger. **Author's pass on the same day:** the title flows as one line where it fits ("THE PEPPER STORY" on every phone from 375 up; the component's forced break was leaving STORY alone) — STORY stays Paprika, the two-line convention's colour, where the frame paints it Chili; the naming sentences ("The name says the whole idea out loud…") now open ¶2 so on phones they follow the timeline card, as drawn (three paragraphs kept; copy doc updated); the founder card's quote mark → text gap is 16 on phones; the ledger header keeps the words (TWELVE YEARS — the author's read: words set the header apart from the digit rows below, the frame's "12" would not); the frame's "make it delicious" typo is fixed in Figma, in the `timelineMobile` component and its seven instances.

(author, Sep 2026); every pill now clears the inset down to 375. **Team — rows, not a second rail (author, Sep 2026).** The first pass stacked two horizontal rails (wall drift at 240, then the eight cards as a snap rail) and the author found the double rail awkward; a 2-column card grid had been tried and rejected (167px cards too narrow for the card's anatomy). So the chip now carries an open state on phones: chevron turns to point down (transform on the spring), border and text hold Paprika while `aria-expanded` is true (flat curve). **Open (mobile About):**
- ~~Desktop connector exports~~ — done; both breakpoints carry the nine seams.
- **Page rendered 453px wide on a phone (found Sep 2026, fixed).** The "Write to the team" dialog is `position: fixed`, 558 wide and centred; hidden or not, a fixed element that pokes past the viewport edge makes WebKit grow the *layout* viewport to its right edge (Chromium doesn't), so the header, the cloud and every full-width block laid out at 453 on a 375 phone. Capped at `calc(100vw − 32px)`. Rule to carry: **no fixed or absolute element may be wider than the viewport at any breakpoint, hidden included** — `#page { overflow-x: clip }` only protects the flow. The dialog itself still has no phone design (its footer row overflows at 326 wide); it is desktop-first like the contact form was, and wants the bottom-sheet treatment the reserve drawer got.
- ~~Hero anchoring on tall phones~~ — judged in the browser at both ends (SE, 15 Pro Max): bottom-anchored stays; the home hero keeps its centred block. Two heroes, two rules, both deliberate.
- Location: the frame draws no "Get directions" button and a "FIND THE PEPPER" eyebrow; the build keeps the button (a map with no route out is a dead end) and the desktop eyebrow.
- 🔶 **Live-text connector prototype (Sep 2026) — the SVG rule under test.** The Figma `SectionLink` set (212:2442) rebuilt as HTML text: `template-parts/components/section-link.php`, the `.section-link` block in `components.css`, `section-link.js` (fits each word's font size to the container after fonts load, so the width rule holds without per-word exports), and the About ground mapping in `about.css` → Connectors → live text. Switched on by `$live_text = true` in `components/connector.php` (moved there Sep 2026 when Visit adopted the device — one flag now decides SVG-vs-text for **both** fixed-composition pages, which is the point: the rule should not land on one page and not the other); flip it to compare against the SVGs. Built to the component's numbers, not the site's: Molot 75% / 4%, word at **50%** with a 0.5px Mushroom rule (the site never dimmed the word), dark reflection 30%, light reflection **100%**; strokes Cream 1 / Paper 1 / Olive 2 / Lime 1, the Peppercorn fade in the dark counters (invisible on a flat ground, as in Figma). Known deltas to judge: CSS `text-stroke` is centred where Figma's is outside (a Figma 1px reads ~0.5px outside here); the stroke stays 1px at every size, so phones run ~3× the weight of the scaled SVGs (the SectionLinkMobile look the phone SVG set was rejected for); the Mushroom rule now sits under every word, dark and light. Wording comes from the connector's `alt`. If it stays, the Width-matching rule and "Must be SVG" need rewriting and the home and menu pages need the same part.

Night keeps its own rule (1 on phones since Sep 2026, 0.3 on tablets and desktop). Heads only: the menu page's upright foot words borrow the same class for their 50% and are left alone. About and Visit needed nothing — their live-text reflections are already at 1 on light grounds. Desktop keeps 50%. This raises the floor; it does not fix the colour — the item below stays open.
- 🔶 **Light-ground reflections are near-invisible — a colour problem, not an export problem (found Sep 2026).** The author re-exported the whole About set from the desktop component; rasterised and compared, the new files carry the same ink as the old within a few percent (the set was re-installed under the template's stems, `desktop/` folder and the two stale files removed). The asymmetry is in the component: on a dark ground the reflection is Paper (17:1 at full, 2.6:1 at the desktop 30%); on a light ground it is **Lime on Parchment — 1.29:1 at full, 1.15:1 at the 50% the rule applies**, and a hairline at that contrast cannot be dimmed into visibility. Same colour on the home and menu pages by day (`atSweetPepper-top` etc. are Lime too), so this is site-wide. Candidates measured on Parchment at 50% / 100%: Olive (the word's own colour) 1.8 / 3.7; Avocado 1.6 / 2.8; Dark Olive 3.0 / 12.8; Lime stays 1.15 / 1.3. Prototyped in the browser with a CSS darken stand-in: **Olive** reads as the word's ghost and lands at 3.7:1 on phones where the rule runs full — the recommended recolour of the `state=reflection, theme=light` variant, then re-export the four About light reflections (wordOfMouth, backToTheFirstPour, theUsualSuspects, makeYourselfAtHome) and the home/menu day sets. Dark Olive at 50% is the closer numeric mirror of the dark side but reads as ink, not a reflection. Open — author's pick.

Measured in Molot at 4% tracking, a phrase lands mid-set at 17–18 characters (ROOM FOR ONE MORE, THE USUAL SUSPECTS) and at the set's top at 13–15 (WORD OF MOUTH, BETTER TOGETHER): IT'S YOUR TURN (14) ≈ 102, NOW IT'S YOUR TURN / SEE YOU AT THE BAR (18) ≈ 76, YOUR TURN TO DROP BY (20) ≈ 66, THE REST IS UP TO YOU (21) ≈ 66. **Picked: NOW IT'S YOUR TURN** (author, Sep 2026), wired at the Location foot. ## Tablet — About page (768–991, built Sep 2026)

About had **one** breakpoint before this pass — a single `≤767` block — so the band ran the desktop layout whole. Unlike the home page, which was merely awkward at these widths, About was losing content.

**The page's signature fault is a fixed pixel column inside a fluid one.** Four instances, and naming them together is the point, because each one failed differently and only the first two looked like bugs:

| | fixed value | what it did in the band |
|---|---|---|
| `.about-team__card` | `width: 260px` | 4 of 8 people clipped away at 768–834, 2 up to 1152 |
| `.about-story__founder` | `flex: 0 0 448px` | narrative squeezed to 240px, ~30 characters a line |
| `.location__map` | `width: 600px` | copy column down to 88px; the headline ran 18 lines, one word per line |
| `.about-how-it-feels__quotes` | `flex: 0 0 540px` | 540 is a *width* on desktop; stacked it became a **height**, pinning ~200px of content in a 540px column |

The last one is the one to remember: **a flex-basis changes which axis it governs when the container's direction flips.** Nothing about the declaration looks wrong when you read it.

### Two fixes that belong at every width, not to the band

- **The team card's inactive face was hidden by clipping, not by hiding.** Both faces sat in normal flow; the second was invisible only because the card is `height: 392px; overflow: hidden`. It was therefore `display: flex`, `visibility: visible`, not `aria-hidden` and not `inert`, one card-height below the first — so a screen reader read all eight hidden messages and keyboard Tab landed on a button nobody could see (8 phantom tab stops → 0). It also made a fluid card width impossible, which is how it surfaced. `.is-active` turns the face back on; the phone block re-declares `display: grid` for its accordion, so phones are untouched.
- **The card is now `width: 100%; max-width: 260px`.** A fixed width inside `repeat(4, 1fr)` cannot shrink — the grid stayed 4 × 260 + 3 × 24 = 1112 whatever the column was, and `#page { overflow-x: clip }` swallowed the remainder with no scrollbar. Both of these were live on **desktop**; neither had been noticed.

### What the band does, section by section

- **Hero** — the phone's 2 + 3 bento. Five cards in one row left them ~137px, narrower than their own Lemon caption pills; the phone grid gives **362–446 wide / 237–293 narrow** across the band, against 205 for all five on desktop. **Second pass (author, 17 September 2026) — the floor was 76px too low.** Below 992 the header is a fixed 76px bar and the body carries a 76px offset, but the band kept the desktop `min-height: 100vh` on top of it; the phone block had subtracted the bar (`100dvh − 76`), the tablet block never had. So the connector on the hero's floor sat **76px below the fold at 820 and 991 and 130 below at 768** while the content itself ended 12–76px *above* it — a full-viewport hero whose last line could never be seen without scrolling. The two floor lines now live in a `≤991` block shared by phones and tablets; the Visit hero takes the same correction (invisible there, its stacked cards being taller than any tablet, but correct). Measured after: on the fold at 820 × 1180; 39 below at 991 × 1200 and 90 below at 768 × 1024, where what remains is the desktop rhythm — the container's 80px top padding and the connector's 80px drop, where phones run 24 / 48. **Open, deliberately:** the author wants the word on the fold at every portrait tablet but will refine the band's typography first, and the rhythm follows the type; the two numbers are the lever once it is settled. Two rhythm calls made now: the text block's flex `gap` (20 on desktop) goes in the band — it was stacking on the lead's own `<p>` margin, putting the headline 40 from its lead and 20 from its eyebrow; the elements' margins carry it (headline → lead 20, lead → bento 68) — and the eyebrow gets **12px** under it, since with the gap gone it sat on the headline with only the line-height as air.
- **Concept** — the phone's rows. `__inner` and `__text` dissolve with `display: contents` and the pieces order themselves. The picker keeps its bleeding Soft Peppercorn band, bled with **the same `clamp()` `.container` uses** rather than the phone's fixed `-16px`, so it lands on the gutter at every width in the band (verified edge-to-edge at 768 / 834 / 991).
- **How it feels** — cloud above the card, as phones stack it; side by side the cloud got 148–315px against the 532 it is placed for and the words collided. **One quote card, not three** (author, Sep 2026): `how-it-feels.js` carries `ONE_CARD_BP = 992`, deliberately separate from `MOBILE_BP`, because `MOBILE_BP` also gates the cloud's drift amplitude and its centre-on-commit — the band keeps the desktop cloud and changes only how many quotes are in view, since the cloud now sits above the rail rather than beside it. **"Add your word" sits left on the grid start in the band (author, 17 September 2026)** — the single-button rule (Grid → Tablet); its desktop right-alignment belonged to the row where card and cloud sat side by side, and phones give it the full width anyway.
- **Perks** — 3 × 2. Six fixed 120px stamps at 28px gaps need 860px and the band has 736–903, so the sixth dropped alone onto a second row. Six divides evenly by three, which is also the phone frame's arrangement.
- **Story** — the phone layout, **moved rather than copied**: the whole Story run was lifted out of the `≤767` block into its own `@media (max-width: 991px)`, so there is one copy to maintain instead of two that drift. One rule in that run was shared with Guests (`.section-headline-group`); it was split and the Guests half stayed behind, so Guests is unchanged. The band therefore gets the frame's order — title → founder → ¶1 → year control → ¶2, ¶3 → ledger — with the segmented control replacing the heat-line axis and the ledger on the ticket's vertical rows.
- **Team — two columns of the photo card (author, Sep 2026).** Eight members divide evenly into two rows of four and raggedly into three, which left a hole at the foot of the section; three-up was the first proposal and the even division is what decided it. Cards run 356 × 487 at 768 to 440 × 571 at 991, against the designed 260 × 392 — squarer, and about 940px more page than a three-up. Bought because faces are the point of this section.
- **Guests** — unchanged, 4-up at 166–208px.
- **Careers and Perks copy** — two `white-space: nowrap` desktop assumptions removed. The careers meta was cut mid-word in a 229–285px card; the perks one-liner is an intrinsic 677px and painted **139px past the viewport at 768**, 82 at 834, 24 at 900 — the sentence simply ran off the right edge. Phones already set both to `normal`. The perks one only surfaced once the team overflow stopped masking it in the audit.
- **Location** — stacked, with the phone's two-line title split. The map takes **`height`, not `min-height`** — see below.
- **Entrance** — **21:9**, the phones' value and the next ratio up the sanctioned list (Image ratios). The desktop 4:1 flattened to a 184–226px strip here; 21:9 gives 315–387.
- **Visit CTA** — the two buttons split the row with `flex: 1 1 0`, the same idiom as `.section-ctas .btn` and `.hero-ctas .btn`, so all three CTA pairs in the band behave alike.

### Two mechanics worth carrying to other pages

- **`height: 100%` needs a *definite* parent height.** The map iframe is `height: 100%`; on an auto-height box that does not resolve and the iframe falls back to the **HTML default of 150px**, so the map painted a 150px strip inside a 500px well. Desktop escapes it because the row's `align-items: stretch` makes the height definite — a column does not, and `min-height` never does. The phone block had used a flat `height` all along. Found by the author; the tablet build had reintroduced it.
- **A rotation's vertical overhang is a function of width.** The quote card sits 1° off true, and `sizeViewport()` measured `offsetHeight`, which ignores transforms — so it reserved the same room for a 364px phone card (3px overhang) as for an 897px one in the band (8px), against the viewport's fixed 4px padding, and the tilted top-right and bottom-left corners clipped. Fixed in the measurement, not the padding: `getBoundingClientRect()` returns the rotated box, so the room scales by itself. Padding would have needed a different value at every width and would have fought the border-box maths.

### Cost and opens

The band runs **13,985–15,125px** against 10,577 on desktop — the largest stacking cost on the site, on its longest page. Accepted, as on the home page.

- **The founder photo is now full width in the band** — **724 × 724** at 768, 891 × 891 at 991 (12px inside the column: its wrapper carries the `padding-right: 12px` that lets the quote card overlap it). Desktop runs 436. It is the phone treatment scaled up and reads as a poster; capping it around 560 flush left is a one-line change if that is too much.
- The Concept, How it feels and Story layouts here are **the phone frames widened, not drawn**. Nothing in Figma covers 768–991 for this page; the numbers were settled in the browser.
- **The hero's foot word on the fold at every portrait tablet** — wanted, deferred until the band's typography is refined (author, 17 September 2026); see the Hero bullet for the numbers and the lever.

## Tablet — Visit page (768–991, built Sep 2026)

Unlike About, Visit already had a `≤991` block written with tablets in mind — its own comment reads *"Tablets: badges over the map, the map at the full width"* — and the audit found **no clipping and no starved text column** at any tablet width. So the strategy here was not "adapt the phone layout" but "find the handful of desktop rules that stop being true", and the page needed far less than About did.

### What the band does

- **Hero** — the frame takes `flex: 1 0 auto` (was `flex: none` in the first pass). It is `flex: 1 1 0; min-height: 0; overflow: clip` on desktop, so content taller than the flex space was silently cut instead of pushing the hero taller: the contacts card lost **166px at 768, 167 at 834, 169 at 900, 171 at 991** — the email row's caption and everything under it. `none` stopped the clipping but let the foot word float mid-section on any viewport taller than the stack; `1 0 auto` grows to the 100vh floor and never shrinks below content. The hero message also drops the desktop `gap: 48px`, which stacked becomes 48px of air between a headline and its own lead. **The two cards are a centred 80% column (author, Sep 2026) — a recorded break of Grid → Short copy ("never centered").** Three layouts were built and measured before it: the full-width stack (ticket and card 720–943 wide; the contact rows ran the trailing action too far from its handle and a receipt-proportioned slip became a banner), a capped ticket flush left ("feels wrong" — a small left-aligned object in a wide hero), and the desktop row kept down to 768 with the phone card skin (336 / 321 at 768 — one screen, but both columns too narrow). 80% of the tablet column — 577 at 768, 608 at 820, 711 at 991 — was the author's compromise between rows too spacious and columns too narrow; the title block and the running rail stay on the gutter, so the cards are visibly a centred column against a left-aligned headline. The percentage is authored against the tablet column, not the 1120 one, so it scales with the band rather than against it. **The phone rail replaces the desktop band across the whole 768–991 band.** The desktop band exists to be half-covered — at 1280 the contacts card sits on its right half — and wherever the overlap goes (below 1120) that half is exposed as empty Lime. A static band with a lead word and two pills hard on the left gutter fought the centred column; the rail — no lead, no gutters, the pair running edge to edge — softens it (author). Its tilt margin became width-based, `0.8727vw` (width × sin 1° ÷ 2: 3.5px at 402, the Figma value; 6.7 at 768; 8.6 at 991). Rail → ticket runs 60px in the band against the phone's 16 — the stack keeps the desktop 36px drop under the card's pin plus the content's 24px top padding — accepted. 992–1119 keeps the desktop band and the full-width stack. **The hero's gutter is the site ramp now**, not a fixed 24: the hero message, band and content sat 8px right of the Location headline at 768 and 20px left of it at 991. *Found on the way:* the ≤1119 block came after the phone block in source order and its `transform: none` was cancelling the phone card's documented 1° rest tilt (Mobile — Contacts); the reset is scoped `768–1119` now and phones rest at 1° again.
- **Location** — badges run full width under the map, two-up (356 / 381 / 440 cells, no name truncating). That matches what the phone block has always done — `order: 2` plus a two-column grid — so the band follows it rather than inventing a third arrangement. *Second pass:* the list had kept its desktop `flex: 0 0 360px`, a width in the row that became a **360px height** in the stacked container (Responsive faults, 2). Chrome stretched the three grid rows to fill it — 121 / 103 / 103 — and Safari left them at natural height and showed the remainder as a gap under the grid; one fault, two browsers, two symptoms. `flex: none`, and **every cell now takes the tallest row's height** (`grid-auto-rows: 1fr`, 60px — the active badge's, with its hint line), so switching routes never re-flows the grid and no cell is stretched by its neighbour; the map container dropped from 824 to 675. Columns are `minmax(0, 1fr)`, as on phones, so a long name can ellipsise instead of widening a column.
- **Good-to-know slip** — full width on the section ground. **The on-map placement was built and rejected** (author, Sep 2026): it sat on the embed's own controls, and — the part that settles it — the scalloped edge is a **cut-out in the section ground**, so over map tiles it rendered as Parchment blobs. A component whose edge is a hole in the background cannot be moved onto a background it does not own. Full width also needed the internal `space-between` to go: right in a 348px card where label and note nearly touch, it stranded the divider mid-card across 736–903.
- **Entrance photo** — the `≤767` 21:9 band (`img` 1441:72476) now runs to 991: **768 × 329 / 834 × 357 / 991 × 425**, and the closure's inline 3:2 photo steps aside as it does on phones. The CTA's head connector goes with it — the rule moved from `≤767` to `≤991` rather than being duplicated, because *the band carries the seam and the reflection goes* is a consequence of the band, not of the width.
- **CTA** — the button pair splits the row (`flex: 1 1 0`), matching `.section-ctas .btn`, `.hero-ctas .btn` and `.about-visit-cta__buttons .btn`. And `.visit-cta__body` had no margin reset, so the browser's default `1em` on a `<p>` sat on top of `.visit-cta__text`'s `gap: 24px` — the body ran 40px from the headline where the rest of the column ran 24. Fixed at the base rule; it was wrong at every width.

### Two bounds that are derived, not chosen

- **The badge column is pinned, the map flexes.** The fixed width moved *from* the map (`736px`) *to* the badges (`360px`). The badge column is the one with a hard content requirement — its names must not ellipsise — while a map is legible at any size, so the column that cannot give is the one that is pinned. With the map pinned instead, the badges were squeezed to 144–300 between 992 and 1279 and **five of the six names truncated**; now none does at any width, and 1280 is unchanged at 360 + 24 + 736 = 1120.
- **The hero split stacks below 1120.** Its two columns are `flex: 0 0 548px` and `width: 524px` — 548 + 48 + 524 = 1120, exactly the design column, so it only ever fitted at 1280 and the contacts card hung **213px past the viewport at 992**. Both columns here *can* give, so both shrink from their own basis; the bases sum to the 1072 available at 1280, keeping 548 / 524 exact. That fixed the overflow but not the second fault: the card is pulled up over the state band by the header's −40px margin, and as the columns shrink its left edge walks left across the chips. Rectangle-intersection testing shows it covers "KITCHEN'S CLOSED" from 992 (61 × 25px) to 1119 (19 × 25) and is clear from **exactly 1120**. Per-component bound, like the home page's section header at 1152; it does not move the page's band, since the location and CTA splits are fluid from 992. *The bound is the overlap's, not the row's: keeping the row below 1120 without the overlap was built and measured (see What the band does → Hero) and rejected on width, so the stack stands.*

### Copy

Sovetskaya's hint is **"Past the fountains"** — the build had drifted to the longer "Across Andropova, past the fountains"; the shorter line is what `visit-page-copy.md` already specifies. Bogoyavlenskaya's hint is restored to **"And the old Kremlin"** (author, Sep 2026), overruling `visit-copy-review-en.md` line 112, which had recommended omitting it as "incomplete"; that note should be marked settled rather than left contradicting the build.

🔶 **"Sovetskaya sq." — a consistency call, not a constraint.** Measured in the 360 badge column, where the name cell is 172px: "Bogoyavlenskaya Square" needs **195** and genuinely does not fit, so its "sq." is a necessity; "Sovetskaya Square" needs only **148** and fits with room, so its "sq." is there to match. That makes a workaround into house style by imitation — the original abbreviation is recorded as *"stays until the author settles it"*. If both should read in full, a **384px** badge column clears the 195 and neither needs abbreviating, trading the Figma 360 for correct copy.

## Tablet — Menu page (768–991, built Sep 2026)

**The band takes the phone menu page whole, widened rather than copied** (author, Sep 2026: *single section across the whole band is just simpler*). One reading model below 992 and one above; rotating an iPad to landscape (1024+) changes the chrome and the columns, not how the page is read. This is the one page that does not follow Grid → Tablet's "phone chrome over desktop content", and the measurements are why.

**Measured before the pass**, desktop layout under phone chrome: the hero nav is a fixed 440, so the photo stack was **182px at 768** (249 at 834) beside a 200px description; the deal is a fixed 352 in a title-row slot of 226–283 and ran **59px into the "Tea & Coffee" headline** at 768; the two dish columns were **344–428, narrower than the 370 a phone gives the same rows**; the jump tab (44 deep, hidden only below 768) sat **28px over the rows** at the 16px gutter, while the Reserve tab was already hidden at ≤ 991; and a tablet has no hover, so the side-nav's one word carried both preview and commit — the touch exception's case. Page length was not the problem (13,656 / 16,159 against desktop's 13,791), so the two-column, all-sections layout was a live option; **the derived split for it is 838** (two 370 columns + 48 = 788 of content, reached at 838) and was not taken. Single-section: 6,371 / 5,855 at 768.

- **Hero** — the phone hero across the band; its fixed 16px gutters are now `--gutter` (variables.css — the same ramp `.container` uses, so the stack lines up with the section below at every width). **The photo runs 21:9 in the band** — a recorded exception to Image ratios (21:9 is "never used for content cards", and this photo is one: the preview and the link). Judged in the browser against the alternatives: 3:2 at the full column is 736 × 490 at 768 and **895 × 597 at 991, where the hero overruns one viewport by 68px** (the home page's stacked-photo open, made worse); 4:1 turns the Manhattan into a rim; 16:9 is the excluded middle. 21:9 fits one viewport at every width (728 × 312 → 895 × 384) with 73–120px of slack above the opener, which the centred block absorbs. A flush-left cap at ~560 was rejected by the author. **Each hero photo carries a focal point** — `focus` in `inc/menu-sections.php`, CSS `object-position`, set inline by PHP and by `menu-hero.js` on every swap. Only the vertical value matters: the crop keeps 64% of a 3:2 photo's height, so `50% 30%` keeps the top (Soups' bowl), `50% 70%` the bottom (Kids' plate). Phones and desktop stay 3:2 and ignore it.
- **Jump-nav** — the edge tab hides at ≤ 991, the Reserve tab's bound (menu-jump-nav.css). The panel stays the desktop 448px drawer, opened from the hero's button; its words preview the hero and the active word drops its arrow, as on phones — `menu-jump-nav.js` gates on 991 now.
- **Sections** — one at a time (`menu-single-section.js` at 991), the rail sticky under the 76px header, words at 36, the current word at the gutter (`alignRail()` reads the rail's own padding instead of a hard 16). The top block bleeds with `--gutter`; its 7:3 photo *is* 21:9 (the same fraction), 329 tall at 768, 425 at 991. Subsections in one column at 736–903. **The deal at full width with its content flush left** — the desktop `space-between` was drawn for a 348 card and stranded the text at the far end of a 736 coupon.
- **Highlights — 3 × 2 grid** (author), 24px gap, cards 229 at 768 → 285 at 991, at or above the desktop rail's 262 for most of the band; desktop link labels. The PHP's alternating tilt classes make a checkerboard by themselves in a three-wide row, so only the phone's 2-up keeps the explicit pattern.
- **Pairing station** — the phone rhythm (the tail reflection flush at the top, 48 / 24) and **the phone's stacked result** (author, Sep 2026): the desktop row was built first — 344 + 344 at 768 — and rejected as cramped, and because the 48px between photos and ticket had no right value against 16–44px page margins. Photo pair full width above the ticket (364 squares at 768, 447 at 991), ticket with 8px radii and the 48px top inset, and **the phone's colours with it** — Olive "x", Parchment night ticket (author: colours follow the layout, not the device). Tags keep the desktop wrap (two rows); only the phone's tag rail stays below 768. The section no longer fits one screen (1,091 tall at 768) — accepted on a tablet.
- **Entrance photo, Location** — already the shared 768–991 block (location.css). The phone-only "Come sit with us" closer stays phone-only; the band ends on Location and the footer, as desktop does.

**Accepted for now (author, Sep 2026):** the deal at full width flush left; 992–1119, where the desktop poster still runs with a 441px photo at 1024 (only a narrowed desktop window hits it).

**Open (tablet menu):**

- **Tablet type styles — set (author, 18 Sep 2026; `design.md` §3.3).** Display 64 and H1 48 in the band, everything else holds; one `@media (min-width: 768px) and (max-width: 991px)` block in `variables.css`. The Highlights and picker titles now run 48 (one line each at 768); the rail words stay 36. The Figma `Typography` collection still wants its Tablet mode.
- **Focal points — final review.** The hero values in `inc/menu-sections.php` were chosen from a contact sheet, not on a device; the section-header photos have `--img-pos` wired but unset, so their 21:9 top-block crops are centred. Both wait for the final review pass.
- **Night connectors** run at the desktop's 30% in the band (phones go to 100% because the scaled stroke reads grey) — check at 736–903.

## Mobile — Menu page (built Sep 2026, layout and structure)

From `menu-browser-day` 2109:128382, as drawn; components (deal, add-ons, highlight card, picker ticket, dish rows) still render their desktop anatomy squeezed to 370 and get their own pass next. Both states × both themes verified in headless Chrome at 402.

**Hero** (`menu-one-photo-hero-kitchen-day-stacked-mobile` 2109:130201). **One viewport, as the home hero (decided Sep 2026):** nav (76, fixed) + hero = 100dvh; the opener and the content travel as one block centred in the slack, the connector sits on the floor; short phones grow (375×667 overruns by ~23px, content-driven). The side-nav panel has no room on a phone, so the hero is one column: a **flush-left Lime button** ("More plates" / "More pours" — see Open) with the author's **kebab glyph** (`assets/icons/kebab.svg`; the build inlines a `c-kebab.svg` currentColor twin so it takes the button's Peppercorn instead of the file's Lemon) that opens the jump-nav panel, the photo stack at the full 370 (sheets fanned into an 8px bottom-right pocket, photo level; **two fan recipes alternate by section** as the components draw — Salads, Soups, Desserts, Beer and No Buzz put Chili mid-slot peeking bottom-left, Lemon at the back), the description, and a **full-width primary that commits** to the section on show. **Its label and glyph are per section (Sep 2026)** from the Figma `menuFood` / `menuBar` variables as recorded in `menu-hero-copy.md` (`ctaLabel`, `Icon`; the author's refinements there win — Salads, not "Salad Bar"), so the button reads "Sandwiches & Bagels" with the burger, "For Kids" with the fork, "No Buzz" with the glass; the glyphs are the file's own `Icons` set, inlined as currentColor twins (`assets/icons/c-*.svg`). Captions and descriptions come from the same source; the Cocktails photo is now the Manhattan (`manhattan-3-2.jpg`, caption "Manhattan"), No Buzz the berry smoothie (`smoothie-1.jpg`) and Infusions the cranberry glasses (`infusions-lenya-11.jpg`, caption "Berry festival" kept for now), in the hero and in their section headers (dedicated 4:1 crops for Cocktails and Infusions — see Image ratios → Section-header crops). Bar hero description is **Parchment** (`menu-bar-mobile` 2175:68888) — at every width since Sep 2026 (desktop night used to keep Mushroom). **Section eyebrows reviewed (Sep 2026):** Kids "favorites they finish", Lunch "weekdays 12pm – 4pm" (pm for the EN audience; a documented exception to the clockless numeral style), Desserts "always room for something sweet", Infusions "Home-made, since 2014"; the other twelve already matched the Figma `Subtitle` values. The room wordmark gives way to the **"food menu" / "drinks menu" connector** at the hero's foot (the existing desktop SVGs, `foodMenu` / `drinksMenu` + their `-reflection` twins, first use) and its reflection opens the next section on the next section's ground. *Rule check:* the wordmark rule says "always filled"; on phones it is replaced, not outlined, so the rule stands — the connector is a connector (Section connectors → scale register).

**Jump-nav on phones — the drawer previews, the button commits (decided Sep 2026).** The left-edge tab is hidden below 768px — a phone edge has no margin for it (the Reserve-tab finding, Top nav → Mobile). The panel opens only from the hero button and is built from `kitchen-drawer-day` 1341:49297: the sheet fills the viewport with the **Lime edge on the left** (8px, top-left corner rounded 4), padding 48 / 72 / 48 / 48, the word list at 16 and the door 48 below, centred; close × at the frame's spot in a 40px target. **Picking a word swaps the hero** (photo, caption, description, and the commit button's label and anchor) and closes the panel — no scroll, no hash. That restores the touch exception's two channels exactly: the drawer word is the preview, the full-width button is the commit, and tapping the already-shown word is never a dead end because the button is still there. On desktop the same panel still jumps. **The door does not preview (decided Sep 2026):** on phones it stays a link to the other menu page — it carries an arrow, and previewing a bar section on the food page would show a hero for a list that isn't on the page; this also means the component's `drinksCoffee` state has no route and isn't built. Consequence applied: the **active word drops its trailing arrow on phones** — settled: a filled word, no arrow, no glyph (a per-meal glyph on the active word was tried and didn't help) (the arrow means "this word is the action"; the frame draws one — re-sync). The panel opens showing the hero's section, not the section in view. Between sections the rail below does the jumping.

**Sections — one at a time (author's prototype, built Sep 2026)** (`menu-food-mobile-day` 2109:130225). On phones the page shows **a single menu section**; the other eight (six on the bar side) stay in the DOM — server-rendered, one URL, deep-linkable — but are hidden until chosen. The section on show follows the hero's section (a drawer word previews the hero and the page follows, no scroll), the **rail** in the section headline, the highlight-card links and the URL hash; each switch writes the hash (`replaceState`) and, when it came from a link, rides the house spring to the section top under the header. Reason: nine stacked sections made the phone page ~17,500px; it is now ~6,000 (bar: 22,000 → 4,700). Consequences applied: every section now sits between the same neighbours, so the striping is fixed (body on the page ground, top block on the surface) instead of positional; the **nine per-section connector words give way to one** — "try the match maker" — after whichever section is on show, with its reflection opening the picker, as the frame draws. Desktop is untouched (all sections, positional striping, per-section connectors).

One column. Each section opens with a **top block on the surface ground** (`--section-top-bg`, so the block reads as the seam under Highlights or the hero reflection): the section photo full-bleed at 7:3 (radius 4, caption inset 16) → the **rail** → the eyebrow, closing with 8px bottom radii. Then, on the page ground: the deal at full width, the subsections as one column at 24px, the tail connector. Section rhythm 36px to the connector (Figma 24 + 12).

**The rail replaces the headline — and is the page's navigation on phones.** The section's `<h2>` sits in place inside a horizontal snap rail of all the section words in **canonical order** (Figma `carousel-food` 1409:50231): current word filled (the headline's own colour), the rest outlined in the hero nav's strokes (Olive by day, Lime by night) — outline → fill, the one state vocabulary, at reading size, so it stays inside the state register. One component at both widths (`menu-section-rail.php`): on desktop only the `<h2>` renders. **Position:** the rail is scrolled so the current word sits at the 16px gutter — earlier sections are reachable by scrolling left, the next one peeks at the right edge (Lunch: Breakfast at −202, Lunch at 16, the frame's own numbers); the last words (Desserts, Kids) sit where the rail's end lets them. *(Replaces the build's earlier wrap-around order, which made the previous section unreachable — author, Sep 2026.)* **Sticky — decided Sep 2026:** the rail sticks under the fixed header while its section scrolls; the eyebrow scrolls away. Same element, no new device; it answers the discoverability and dead-zone concerns — a one-section page needs a tab bar and this is it. Cost accepted: 54px under the 76px header — **8 + the 38px words + 8 (author, 20 Sep 2026):** the band had 8 above the words and 0 below, which read as a cropped strip once stuck; the bottom now equals the top. Unstuck, that puts the eyebrow 8px further from the word (the top block grows 46 → 54 for the rail) — accepted; the frame's 46 is the figure to re-sync. **The stuck rail trembled on iPhones — found and fixed, confirmed on the author's phones 21 Sep 2026.** While the page scrolled, the stuck rail wandered ±4 CSS px against the fixed header, in Safari and Chrome alike (both WebKit); a Mac never showed it. Measured in the author's screen recording, stepped frame by frame: the header's wordmark on rows 363–421 of every frame, the rail's word anywhere from 540 to 552, back to 544 at rest — a sticky layer placed a frame late, in proportion to scroll speed. **The cause was a section-level `overflow-x: clip`:** `reveal.js` put `.has-reveal` on every section that had any reveal, the menu sections among them, and on WebKit a clipping ancestor takes a sticky layer off the scrolling thread. That clip exists for one effect only — `mask-right`, the elements that wait off to the right and would widen the page — so it now goes only on a section that holds one (on the menu page: the pairing station and Location, never a menu section). Isolated with temporary `?debug=` switches on the phones (`noreveal` and `noclip` stopped it; the nudge, the photo drift and the parallax were cleared), since removed. `#page` keeps its own clip below 992px — harmless, as the test showed. **Rules to carry:** (1) no `overflow-x: clip` or `hidden` on an ancestor of a `position: sticky` element; (2) never make one element both sticky and a scroll container; (3) compositor-level bugs on iOS are invisible to layout measurements — ask for a screen recording and measure the frames. *Two earlier attempts stay in the build because they are right in themselves, though neither was the cause:* whole-pixel geometry (words on a 38px line → the rail is exactly 54px, not 53.8; the section photo's height rounded to a whole pixel; the row `overflow-y: hidden`), and the rail split in two — the bar (`.menu-section-rail`) is sticky and does not scroll, the row inside it (`.menu-section-rail__nav`) scrolls sideways with the snap and the 16px lead-in. History: `report.md`, 20–21 Sep. A tap on a stuck rail switches the section and lands on its top. Words are plain same-page anchors; on phones `menu-single-section.js` switches the section instead of scrolling the page.

**In the rail every word is the nav word — including the current one (author, 20 Sep 2026; found on the test site).** Three sections carry a longer desktop headline — *Sandwiches & Bagels*, *Pepper's Salads*, *For Little Peppers*. In the rail they now read SANDWICHES, SALADS, KIDS, the same word the guest tapped: a tab that renames itself when chosen reads as a different place, and the long headline filled the row (≈ 400px of 370), so the next word never peeked and the rail stopped looking like a rail. Desktop keeps the long headlines (one `<h2>`, two spans, one hidden per width). Measured after the change: the next word shows at least **104px at 360** and 110px at 402 in every section, both menus. 🔶 Side effect to look at: *Sandwiches* and *Salads* now sit directly above a first subsection with the same word (SANDWICHES / SANDWICHES). **Discoverability — entrance, then an idle nudge (author, built 20 Sep 2026).** The author found even the sticky rail under-noticed: an outlined word at the right edge doesn't say "swipe me". Tried as `?rail=` prototypes on the live page (nudge · entrance · both), chosen: **both**, and the flag is gone. **Entrance** — once per section: when its rail is *where the guest is looking* — as it crosses into the top two thirds of the screen, while the section's photo is still above it — the words after the current one arrive from the right (72px, 80 ms apart, house spring); until then they wait out of sight, so nothing blinks away before it enters. The first pass fired when the rail touched the fold and was over before anyone looked ("noticed on the second glance, and only because I knew what to look for"). Accepted consequence: a guest who pauses with the rail low on the screen sees the current word alone until they scroll on. *(Tuned on the phone, 21 Sep 2026: the entrance first also waited for the scroll to rest — right with a mouse wheel, but a phone fling scrolls on momentum for a second or two, so it played after the rail had stuck and the photo was gone. It now fires on crossing the line: at 63–64% of the screen with the whole photo in view, on a 1500 px/s fling and a slow drag alike.
