The nudge went 5 s → 2.5 s: on a phone the pauses read longer.)* **Nudge** — only for a rail still untouched: every 2.5 s (never sooner than 2.5 s after the entrance) the words start a swipe by themselves, 14px left and back on the spring — left, because that is how a swipe begins and it shows more of the next word. **Both end for the visit at the first touch, scroll or key on any rail** (`sessionStorage`), and a touch on a waiting rail shows its words at once. Nothing runs under reduced motion, above 991px, in a hidden tab or off-screen; without the script the rail is simply there. Legal under Motion language → Idle self-demo, where it is the fourth "stops for good" exception. `src/js/menu-rail-nudge.js`, `menu-section.css`. *Considered and not taken:* a static "›" at the edge (a new glyph where the system has none); filling the next word at low opacity (breaks outline = not current, the site's whole state vocabulary).

**Highlights** (2109:130202): six cards in a **2-up grid** (16px gaps) instead of the rail, title → 24 → grid → 48 → connector, the hero connector's reflection above. Tilts form the frame's checkerboard (+1 −1 / −1 +1 / +1 −1), not the rail's simple alternation. **Night** (menuSection 2194:70367, checked Sep 2026): cards Peppercorn on the Soft Peppercorn section, Cream titles, Lime links, Cream eyebrow — as the build already had; the one addition is the title's second line **"Highlights" in Paprika on phones by night** (the day frame keeps one Chili title; desktop unchanged). Ground: Soft Peppercorn, as the desktop night frame (SeasonalMenuSection 833:34450) draws it — author confirmed Sep 2026; the phone night frame's Peppercorn is the one to re-sync, like the day frame's Paper. Eyebrow **Avocado at every width** (Sep 2026) — the menu page's own eyebrow colour and what both frames of this section draw; it had inherited the home page's Olive from the shared header component. *Ground — decided Sep 2026:* Parchment (the desktop striping), so the connector + reflection sit on a ground change; the frame's Paper is the one to re-sync. Card links take a phone-only short label ("In Soups" — `link_label_mobile`) because "Show in Desserts →" does not fit a 145px column. Titles run one line with an ellipsis as the frame draws two of them.

**Pairing station** (2109:130226, picker `foodPairingContainer-mobile-day` / `-night` 1432:54562 / 1432:53793 — layout only; the content is the desktop's). The reflection of the tail connector sits flush at the section top, 48 to the header, 24 to the picker, 48 to the **"you'll like it" connector, which ends the section** with the entrance photo directly below (the current frame shows it; an earlier one hid it). Tags are a scroll rail **from gutter edge to gutter edge**, and **Shake It! sits between the two photos, where the "x" was (author, 18 Sep 2026)** — the shaker joining the plate and the glass is "Shake & cook" drawn literally, and it frees the row: the rail is 402 wide where the pinned chip left it 240, one tag and a sliver. *History:* the frame drew Shake at the end of the scroll (x≈540 on a 402 phone — invisible on load and after every pick); Sep 2026 pinned "or Shake It!" at the right of the row, which fixed that and starved the tags; five arrangements were mocked on the live page on 18 Sep (sentence line · slot button · second row · the × · wrap) and the × won. Wrapping stays rejected — 735px, the ticket below the fold. **The button** (Figma `shakeItContainer-desktop` / `-mobile` 2437:71646 / 2437:72125, **responsive — "the same on every width" was this document's misreading, never a decision (author, 21 Sep 2026)**; tablet band 768–991: 64 with the Symbol at 32, ring still 4 so the disc is 56 — between two 364–447px photos the 48 read as a full stop; to be judged on the tablets; no Figma frame covers the band): phones and desktop 48px — a 4px ring in the section's ground, a colour block cut out of the photos, around a 40px disc, **Lemon by day (author: keep Lemon; the frame draws Cream), Cream with a 0.5px Lemon hairline by night**, the Symbol at 24px (`design.md` §4 floor; the old chip drew it at 16). States: **hover 52**, **pressed 52** with the house inset shadow, **delay 56** — scale, never size (ratios ×1.0833 / ×1.1667 of the resting size, so the 64px tablet button beats to ~75). By night the disc carries the Lemon glow (0 0 8, the pinned event card's) at rest where there is a hover-capable pointer, and during the delay beat on touch. **No visible "Shake It!" label anywhere (author)** — the copy, the hover and the delay beat do the advertising; the name lives in `aria-label`. *Copy owed: neither subtitle mentions shaking yet.* **Motion:** a tap spins the shaker one turn on the house spring while the ticket re-prints; hover rattles it; on touch the delay state is the idle hint — 2.5 s after the photos are in view, then the 5 s heartbeat, the section's one clock; a tag pick seizes it for 8 s and **the first Shake ends it for the visit** (Motion language → the third stop-for-good exception, same reason as How it feels: a guest who has found it must not be nagged). The rail fades at an end only while there is more that way; the picked tag scrolls to the gutter on load, after a pick and after a Shake (re-measured once Golos has loaded). **Height:** the photos' gap went 26 → 8 (Figma `img` 2437:71711), so they grew 172 → 181 at 402 and the picker runs **631px header to ticket foot (was 622)**; 625 at 390, 618 at 375. *Open: judge on a real phone with the browser chrome showing; the cheapest 12px back is the ticket's bottom padding 36 → 24.* **The photos roll on a pick (author's idea, built 19 Sep 2026):** each frame is a mask and the photos inside it move like a rail, in opposite directions — the plate comes down from the top, the glass up from the bottom, a beat (80 ms) later: the two hands of a shake, and the middle step of a top-down cascade (tag → photos → ticket). Old and new travel together, 8px apart like frames on a strip, 900 ms on `--ease-out-expo` — **the curve the photos entered on, not the spring**: a spring's overshoot would cross the mask's edge and show the ground (the same reason the mask reveals don't take it; the ticket does because its overshoot leaves the mask). Only the images move, on `translate`; the frames stay the reveal engine's. Interruptible like the ticket — the 350 ms lock that used to drop fast picks is gone, the last pick wins. Reduced motion keeps the old crossfade. *Considered and dropped:* replaying the sideways entrance (the picker stands empty for a beat, ticket and photos both away), a wipe outward from the Shake button (right for a Shake, meaningless for a tag pick, whose cause is above), and a slot-machine run through other dishes on Shake (shows pairs that are not the answer, costs over a second, and is gambling imagery at a bar). **Entrance:** the button pops on the seam as the photos meet (+350 ms, the beat the "x" had), centred by offsets — a `translate` centring is wiped by the reveal engine for the length of the entrance (seen 18 Sep: it popped at the right photo's edge, low). **About shows the same six dishes (author, 18 Sep 2026)** — one list, `inc/pairings.php`; its earlier three-dish copy is retired, and with it, for now, the reviewed wording in `about-page-copy.md` (open: fold that wording into the shared list). The two photos stay side by side, 8px apart; the ticket goes full width with **8px radii and a 48px top inset** — and is **Parchment by night on phones** where the desktop ticket is Cream (both as drawn; one of the two to reconcile if the ticket should be one colour everywhere). The night component's own header ("try it yourself", H2 Lime) is not used — the section keeps "find your match!".

**Entrance photo + Location + closer** (2109:130245 / 130246 / 130264, built Sep 2026): the entrance photo full-bleed at 21:9 with the `img` component's 4px radii; **Location on Parchment** (the desktop's Paper stays desktop; Soft Peppercorn by night) at 48 / 34, the copy over a 400px map at 24, and the headline breaking after "heart" — Chili "at the very heart", Paprika "of the best city" — where the desktop breaks after "of the" (one span swaps side per breakpoint). *(21 Sep 2026: the swapping span is gone — the headline is two typed lines shared with About and Visit, "in", not "at"; from 992 the two lines flow as one sentence. Content editing → About fields.)* *(23 Sep 2026: IN THE HEART / OF THE CITY, the typed break at every width, size fitted to the column — Content editing → About fields → Open.)* Then the **phone-only "Come sit with us" closer** on Paper (Peppercorn by night): eyebrow "join the party", H1, the About page's visit copy, and one full-width Reserve button (`primary-green`, phone glyph trailing) that opens the reserve sheet — the frame's earlier two-button version is gone. The desktop menu page has no such section; it is phone-only by design until the desktop says otherwise.

**Desktop side effect, deliberate:** the Kids section's connector is now **"try the match maker"** (was "favorites they'll finish") — it is the one food section whose connector sits on the picker rather than the next section's photo, so it names what follows. Still non-interactive (Section connectors); if it should *link*, that is a rule change to argue first.

**Open (mobile menu):**

- **Opener label and glyph — settled (Sep 2026):** "More plates" / "More pours", kebab glyph. Arrows were tried and read as page navigation; the room glyphs stay on the commit button, where they name the section. The bar component (2175:68888) still reads "More plates" — re-sync. RU twins still owed.
- **Rail discoverability — settled (Sep 2026): the sticky rail.** With one section on show the rail is the main navigation, not a shortcut, so it stays under the header while the section scrolls (author confirmed in the browser). The floating "More plates" alternative is rejected: it collides with the CTAs at the foot of sections (the Reserve finding) and its drawer previews the *hero*, so mid-page it would change something far above the guest or need a second behaviour.
- **Tail connector gap:** 44px above the word today (the frame's 36 plus the subsection's 8px bottom inset), 1px onto the picker. Author to set the final number in the browser; the other phone sections run 48 to their connectors.
- **Commit button label — settled (Sep 2026): "Breakfast"**, as drawn. The connector under the hero already says "food menu", so the button names the section, not the document.
- **Bar connector word — settled (Sep 2026):** "drinks menu" (the `drinksMenu` SVG); doors name contents and the food twin is "food menu". The bar component's "bar menu" is the one to re-sync.
- **Bar hero description colour — settled (Sep 2026): Parchment at every width**, the bar component's value; the desktop rule moved into the drinks-state block (menu-hero.css). Kitchen night keeps Mushroom.
- **Drawer word colour.** The frame outlines the words in Chili (Word list roles → "Day: Chili outline") with Kids in green; the build keeps the desktop panel's Olive strokes (Lime by night), which is also what the hero nav ships with. One of the two is stale — the hero, the panel and the rail should take the same answer.
- **Visit CTA — built phone-only (Sep 2026).** Whether the desktop menu page should end the same way is still the author's call.
- **Highlight titles:** the frame wraps two titles to two lines and truncates others; the build truncates all. Component pass.
- **Highlight card arrow — decided Sep 2026 (author confirmed): keep →.** The card link jumps to a menu section on this page, and every other section jump here — hero words, drawer words, the rail — uses →; the arrow marks "this is the action", not a compass. The one ↓ in the house (Visit → Directions) is a single link pointing at the map directly beneath it. Six ↓ in a grid would read as a download list and would split one action across two glyphs on one page. The wrong-version test: dropping the arrows and making the whole card the link fails on touch — nothing would mark the card as tappable.

## The no-clock rule (hospitality principle)

Good service makes guests lose track of time — so no literal clocks or countdowns in the hero. Schedule is implied by slider position and soft copy ("the kitchen's on — no rush"). Exact times appear only where guest anxiety is real (breakfast cutoff may say "served till noon") and on service pages (menu, contacts).

## The bartender's ticket

A site-wide component: an order slip the interface "prints" to answer the guest — the kitchen–bar handshake made tangible. Motif lineage: the bottle-cap scalloped edge (`design.md` §5.1).

- **Anatomy:** Paper card · small-caps header line (SWEET PEPPER · KIROVA 10, Caption tracking) · the guest's line (Golos Medium, Peppercorn) · dashed rule · the bar's reply (Golos, Deep Chili) · optional chip exit link · bottom edge cut with the bottle-cap scallop.
- **Motion:** it *prints* — slides out from the control that produced it, with the house spring easing. Never fades in like a tooltip. **Built 18 Sep 2026 in the dish picker (About Concept and the menu page's pairing station — one component):** a pick is an order — the slip pulls back up into its slot (220 ms, ease-in), the reply is written while it is out of sight, and it prints again over `--dur-gentle` on `--ease-gentle`; the spring's overshoot runs downward, away from the slot, and reads as paper feeding a touch too far (≈ 9px). The slot is `.dish-picker__card-wrap` itself (`overflow: hidden`), so the paper — card and both scalloped edges — moves inside it on `translate` and nothing else is masked. Interruptible: a new pick starts from wherever the paper is, and the last pick always wins. Reduced motion: text swaps in place, as before. The About entrance print (`mask-down`) rides the same spring — the one mask effect that does, because its overshoot leaves the mask's edge instead of crossing it.
- **Modes:** always Paper, day and night — a receipt is paper regardless of the hour. On dark grounds it's the brightest object in the section, so max one per view.
- **Voice:** the reply is always a person speaking ("unforgettable with a shot of the cranberry infusion"), never a template ("pairs well with X").
- **Used in:** About — the pairing picker prints one (spec: `about-page-copy.md`, The Concept). Candidates later: reservation confirmation, menu specials.
- **Not the footer any more (Aug 2026).** The footer became a saturated Lime band in both modes (Section striping), and Lime out-brights Paper — a slip there loses the "brightest object in view" property the component depends on. Hours, address and "come sit with us" live in the footer as plain type instead. One closing device per page.

## Motion language

One vocabulary across the site, so every interactive section feels like one hand:

- **Outline → fill is the state change.** Slider meal labels, the About chapter rail, the word cloud, the Story timeline's markers, the heat-filling wordmark — "filled" always means active / current / hot. Photographic surfaces state it as **dimmed → full strength**: the mobile hero's daypart tiles unlit the three that aren't selected rather than haloing the one that is (Mobile — home hero). Same grammar, applied to images instead of letterforms.
- **One clock per section.** A single idle rhythm drives everything that moves in a view (About's heartbeat fills a word and turns the quote wheel together). Never two independent idle animations in one section.
- **Idle self-demo, human seizure.** Untouched sections demo themselves (heartbeat, an occasional shake); any interaction seizes the clock and pauses auto-motion for ~8s, then it resumes. **Four stop for good instead (documented exceptions)** — the fourth, since 20 Sep 2026, is the phone menu rail's entrance and nudge, which end at the first touch on any rail (Mobile — Menu page → the rail); the third, since 18 Sep 2026, is the dish picker's Shake It! hint, which ends at the first Shake (Pairing station); the first two: the menu hero's idle sweep (Menu page → Idle behaviour) and, since Sep 2026, **How it feels** — the first touch on a word, a card or the rail ends the heartbeat for the visit, because a guest who has just discovered the interaction and is exploring it must not be interrupted by the demo (author). The 8 s pause there times only the visual release (undim, ease home). Heartbeat 5 s, was 3.5.
- **Scroll tweens take the spring too.** A `scrollTop` animation can't use a CSS easing, so `how-it-feels.js` solves `--ease-gentle` (cubic-bezier 0.33, 0.57, 0.08, 1.19) in JS and rolls the quote rail over 900 ms with it, snap off during the tween — native `scroll-behavior: smooth` was the browser's own curve at its own speed and read harsh. Reuse `gentleEase()` for any future scroll roll.
- **Spring easing** for prints, rolls, and snap-backs (bartender's ticket print, wheel roll); transforms only — never animate layout. *Exception recorded Sep 2026 (author to confirm or narrow): the mobile hero tiles animate `width` / `height` during the tap-to-move FLIP. A transform-only move with counter-scaling would stretch the photo crops mid-flight; a size transition keeps `object-fit: cover` honest. Position still moves on `transform`.* (The build also uses Gentle site-wide, wider than "prints, rolls and snap-backs" — see `report.md` → Motion.)
- **Colour-only transitions take the flat curve, at the house duration.** Background, border and text colour move on `--ease-gentle-flat` over `--dur-gentle`; the springy `--ease-gentle` overshoots past the end value on a colour and flickers. *Swept Sep 2026:* the address chips (`.contacts-chip`, home Contacts + the Visit map bar) were the last holdout on a hand-written `all 0.15s ease` — also an `all`, which animated layout properties nothing intended to move. Chips, buttons and the Visit route badges now share one declaration.
- **prefers-reduced-motion:** drift, parallax, and auto-advance off; state changes (fill, quote swap, ticket content) remain.

### Page-load entrance — home hero (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review on a device)

The load states the engine's answer instead of decorating it. Copy rises (headline behind a stationary mask) → the four tiles deal in from the left inside their own frames, **all unlit**, 120 ms apart in day order → the active tile lights — *dimmed → full strength*, the hero's own state grammar: overlay lifts, keyline draws, the three sheets fan out from behind the photo, the Now pill pops → the foot word rises out of the section's bottom edge. **Last of all (desktop, 2.0 s): the language nudge slides in from the right edge of the column, masked by its wrapper — its dismissal reversed.** It is not part of the composition, so it waits until the composition is finished (author, 18 Sep 2026). Desktop: the composition is complete at ≈ 2 s (headline readable by ~0.5 s, Reserve by ~1 s); the nudge then lands at ≈ 2.8 s. On the bento the tiles lead and the copy follows, because the tiles are the top of that composition.

- **CSS owns it, and it needs no arming.** `backwards`-filled keyframes in `hero.css` start with the first paint; with JS off the server-rendered tile lights itself, and the rest state is the finished state. This differs from the Story's `.is-armed` pattern on purpose: an above-the-fold section armed by a deferred script would paint finished, then hide, then animate.
- **The engine does three small things** (`daypart-engine.js`): swaps the server's breakfast tile for the real daypart with transitions off while the tiles are still hidden (this also removes the old visible jump on load); re-times `--in-light` for the tile it activated; adds `.is-settled` when the last animation ends. A tap during the entrance settles it at once — human seizure. If the script arrives after the tiles have begun to show, the swap plays as an ordinary tap.
- **Easing.** Masked slides (tiles, headline) take `--ease-out-expo`, the hero's curve — a spring's overshoot would pull a photo past its frame and open a gap. Unmasked moves (copy lift, sheet fan, foot word) take Gentle; the pill takes Bouncy; opacity and keyline take the flat curve.
- **Reduced motion:** no entrance at all; the swap is instant.
- **Cost recorded:** the hero tiles and the hero's foot word are now `loading="eager"` — an image parked outside its frame's clip never intersects, so a lazy one would not start loading until it had already slid in empty. They are the first viewport, so eager is right regardless.
- **First-paint theme — closed the same day.** A night page used to paint in day clothes and flip when the deferred engine ran. `inc/daypart-head.php` now prints a few lines of blocking inline JS at the top of `<head>`: it works out the daypart, writes `<html data-now>`, and sets `data-theme="night"` by the engine's own rules (`?menu=drinks`, `?theme=night`, `?daypart=`, else the hour — home only). Checked: the engine makes **zero** theme writes on load. **The hour thresholds now live there and only there** — the engine reads `data-now` and no longer computes the hour; when hours move to the ACF options page, that function is where PHP hands them over. Known small duplication: both the head script and the engine's `daypartData` know that dinner and party are night.

### Page-load entrance — About hero (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review)

The home entrance in the same hand, **minus the lighting step**: About is a fixed composition and none of the five chapter cards is "now", so there is no answer to state. Eyebrow rises → headline rises behind its mask → lead rises → the five cards deal in from the left inside their own frames, 120 ms apart, **in their rest state** (dimmed on desktop, lit on touch) → each caption pill pops as its card lands → BETTER TOGETHER rises onto the connector's Mushroom rule, which stays put from the first paint. Same timings at every width — the content order doesn't change between the row and the 2 + 3 bento.

- **Pure CSS and nothing else**: `backwards`-filled animations in `about.css` (after the hero rules), reusing the home hero's keyframes from `hero.css` — one set for both pages. No script, no arming, no settled state, because nothing here re-triggers. JS off = same entrance; reduced motion = none.
- The card photos are `loading="eager"` (`about/hero.php`) for the reason recorded above: a photo parked outside its frame's clip never intersects.
- **Correction to an earlier note:** the About connectors are the *live-text* prototype (`.section-link`), not SVGs, so the home page's connector parallax — which targets `.section-link-word--reflection img` — does **not** run on About yet. The live-text reflection is flipped with `transform: scaleY(-1)`, so the lag there must use the individual `translate` property or it will un-flip the word. Done with the About scroll pass (below).

### Page-load entrance — Visit hero, and Visit scroll-in (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review)

**The fastest of the three entrances** — Visit is a service page and people come for hours and a phone number, so everything is readable inside ~1 s (slip by ≈ 1.0 s, card by ≈ 1.4 s, foot word last at ≈ 1.7 s). Eyebrow, headline (behind its mask) and description rise → **the Lime status band is laid down left to right, like a strip of tape** → its two state pills pop and each dot lights → **the hours slip prints** from its own top edge on the house spring (the ticket gesture, as the pairing ticket) → the Get in touch card drops in at its tilt, the Chili pin a beat after → YOUR ROUTE TO PEPPER rises.

- **The band is this page's answer** — "can I come now?" — so it plays the part the lit tile plays on Home: the thing the entrance is *for*.
- **Phones:** the running rail takes the band's place and is laid the same way; the whole entrance finishes inside the rail's 2.5 s hold, so the rail stays the hero's one ongoing motion (One clock per section).
- Pure CSS at the end of `visit.css`, `backwards`-filled, the shared hero keyframes plus four of its own (`visit-band-lay`, `visit-print`, `visit-drop`, `visit-dot-on`). Everything that owns a transform — the band's 1°, the card's −1°, the pin, the phone slip — is moved with `translate` / `scale` / `clip-path`, which compose with it; the card's bouncy hover is untouched. No script; reduced motion = none.

**Scroll-in — the quietest plan** (`VISIT` in `reveal.js`). Getting here: eyebrow rises, headline behind its mask, the Lemon Good-to-know slip pops, **the route badges rise one by one as rows — they are controls, so they never slide** — and the map column enters from the right. We're all ears: headline behind its mask, the room photo from the left (`.visit-cta__photo` on desktop, the full-bleed `.visit-band` on phones and tablets — whichever has a box), copy and buttons rise, **the form only rises** — a working surface, as in the home Contacts. Replay from below applies to the map and the photo only. The connector lag already ran here (live-text connectors). **No photo parallax on this page:** three sections, task-driven, and its one photo is the door that already drifts on About.

**Rejected for this page (author, 18 Sep 2026):** a route that draws itself on the map as a badge scrolls in (triggers a state the guest didn't choose; fights the map embeds), hours rolling like a flip clock (no-clock rule; delays the one thing people came for), and the status band as a desktop ticker (scrolling text has lost twice here; the phone rail is the accepted exception).

Checked with seeked frames at 1440 × 900 and 402 × 874 (hero) and the full-page step-through at 1440, 820 and 402 (scroll): everything released, no horizontal scroll, no errors. Not yet on a real device.

### Page-load entrance — Menu hero (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review)

The rule for the whole menu page first: **header furniture moves, the dish lists never do** — 253 rows, time-to-dish is the KPI, and *Menu lists* already says the lists stay a fast reading surface.

The nav sheet slides in from the left edge — **the jump-nav panel's own move**, so "the hero's column coming back" reads in both directions — with its Lime edge a beat behind. The section words rise 40 ms apart, all outlined; then **the opening word fills and takes its arrow** (outline → fill: this page's answer, as the lit tile is Home's and the status band is Visit's). The door pops. The photo slides in inside its frame, the keyline draws, the three sheets fan out from behind it and the pill pops — the Home tile's device, so the Home tile's entrance. The description rises; the room wordmark rises out of the section's bottom edge. Word list readable by ≈ 0.8 s, all of it done by ≈ 1.9 s. **The food ↔ drinks door is a page load, so this entrance is also the room change** (Doors → Motion's crossfade candidate is still open — a cross-document view transition would be the way to try it).

- **Below 992px** the hero is the phone model (no word list, no wordmark): the photo slides in, its sheets only fade — inset 4px with a fraction of a degree, there is nothing to fan — the pill pops, description and commit button rise, and the FOOD MENU / DRINKS MENU connector word rises in the wordmark's place.
- Pure CSS at the end of `menu-hero.css`, shared hero keyframes plus four. **One script gate:** the fill is tied to `.is-active`, which hover moves from word to word, so `menu-hero.js` adds `.is-settled` on the first pointer / focus / touch in the nav, or after 2.6 s — otherwise every hovered word would replay the fill with its load delay. Checked: a hover at 0.6 s settles at once and the hovered word just fills.
- ~~**Found:** the brief's *Idle behaviour* is not built, and the opening frame is the server's default section.~~ **Both built 18 Sep 2026** — Menu page → Idle behaviour → *As built*. The sweep's first hold is counted from this entrance's 2.6 s gate, so the two never overlap.

**Parallax on this page — corrected (author, 18 Sep 2026).** I had claimed the connector lag already covers the menu page. It doesn't: only two real reflections exist here (Highlights' head, the pairing station's head). At all nine food and seven drinks sections **`.menu-section__hero`, the wide photo band, stands where a reflection would** — the foot word of the section above sits directly on it. So the photo band is the head of the pair and carries the lag — built with the section pass, below. *(Second correction: I wrote that the tablet pass had set a focal point per section band. It hadn't — the authored focal points belong to the menu **hero** photo, inline; the bands read `--img-pos`, which nothing sets, so they are all centred today.)*

### Scroll-in — Menu pages (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review)

`MENU` plan in `reveal.js`, food and drinks alike. **Header furniture moves, the dish lists never do.**

- **Every section:** the photo band slides in from the left with its Lemon pill popping as it lands; the eyebrow rises; the title rises behind its mask; the deal slip pops. Sub-heads and rows are simply there.
- **Seasonal highlights:** header as above; the card rail rises as one piece (a horizontal scroller is never dealt card by card).
- **Pairing station:** About's Concept again — plate from the left, glass from the right, the Shake It! button popping on the seam as they meet (it took the ×'s place and its beat, 18 Sep 2026), and the ticket prints (and re-prints on a pick, while the photos roll — Pairing station). **Location band** and **closing CTA** as on About: copy rises, titles behind their masks, the map from the right, the entrance pill pops.
- **Phones and the tablet band** run one section at a time: only the section on show has a box, so the others are never armed and appear finished when switched to. Nothing to wait for between sections.
- **Rides don't deal the page.** A jump-nav or hero-word pick crosses thousands of pixels on the scroll spring. While the page moves faster than four viewports a second nothing plays, anything left entirely above the screen is handed back unplayed, and the section the spring settles on plays as an arrival. Checked from the hero to Desserts (9,671px): Highlights played as the ride set off, then only Desserts. The rule lives in the engine, so it holds on every page.
- **The photo band carries the connector's lag** (see the correction above): the picture drifts ±12% inside its band — `object-position`, so nothing is scaled; the bands are 4:1 / 21:9 crops of much taller photos. It drifts *around* the focal point: the band's position is rebuilt from `--img-x` / `--img-y` (default 50%) plus a registered `--band-drift`, clamped to 0–100% so a focal point near an edge can't pull the photo off its frame. **Those two properties are now the authoring hook for a band's focal point**; `--img-pos` is no longer read on bands where scroll-driven animation is supported.
- **Replay from below** applies to the bands, the pairing photos and the map.
- **Not built:** the bar's notes (no slip exists in the templates yet — when it does, it prints once, `mask-down`); the cross-document view transition for the food ↔ drinks door.

Checked by stepping through `/menu/` and `/menu/?menu=drinks` at 1440 × 900 and `/menu/` at 820 × 1180 and 402 × 874: everything armed was released, no horizontal scroll, no errors; all other pages re-run after the engine change, and Home's down → up → down replay check again.

### Headline swap on tile tap — ScrambleText (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review)

A tap on a tile scrambles the headline into the next one over 600 ms, resolving left to right (`src/js/scramble-text.js`, no dependency). It replaces a hard text swap, so it is a **state change, never idle** — it does not count against One clock per section, and the load swap stays a direct write because the entrance is already moving the headline.

- **Molot is not monospaced, so the pool is the target's own letters** minus the narrow ones (I, punctuation). The scramble is made of the word it is becoming; line width stays within a few px of the resolved line, and RU needs no charset.
- **The box is held.** The target's height is measured in the same frame and the taller of the two is kept as `min-height` for the ride; when both ends are one line the ride is `nowrap`. Sampled every frame at 1440, 402 and 340 wide, including a second tap mid-ride: zero height changes, no horizontal scroll, inline styles released at the end.
- Interruptible (a second tap starts from what is on screen); reduced motion writes the target directly; screen readers get the target at once via `aria-label` for the ride.
- **Open:** the subhead and the menu button's label still swap hard beside it — decide whether that contrast is the point (one thing scrambles, the rest answers) or whether the subhead wants a quick crossfade. Scope stays the home headline: scramble on connectors is impossible (SVG by rule) and on menu/section names it would fight outline → fill.

### Scroll-in entrances — home, below the hero (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review)

One engine (`src/js/reveal.js` + `src/css/reveal.css`), the Story's contract: the server renders the finished state, pre-entrance states exist only once the script has **armed** an element, each element plays **once**, and when it has played both classes are stripped so its own CSS (hover lifts, shadows, transitions) takes over untouched. **No exits** — six sections and six connectors re-triggering on every scroll back is noise; parallax is the only thing that answers scrolling up. Reduced motion: nothing is armed.

- **Templates untouched.** The page's plan lives at the top of `reveal.js` as *effect → selectors*; markup can also opt in with `data-reveal="…"`. Other pages get their own plan the same way.
- **Six effects** (five at first; `mask-down` came with the About pass — it is the ticket's print, the one mask on the Gentle spring). `rise` (copy, chips, buttons, dish rows — 16px + fade, Gentle) · `mask-up` (Molot headlines — the hero headline's move, so H1 and H2 enter alike) · `mask-left` / `mask-right` (containers, masked by their own box — no wrapper: the element is parked a full width away and clipped by the same amount, so the mask edge stays put while it travels) · `pop` (Lemon pills, Bouncy). Masked moves take `--ease-out-expo`, as in the hero.
- **Stagger is by batch, not by numbering.** Whatever crosses the line (12% above the fold) in the same check plays in document order, 120 ms apart. A row of cards deals left to right; the second row of Events deals when *it* arrives.
- **Where direction means something:** Bar is text-left and Kitchen is image-left, so the Bar photo enters from the right and the Kitchen photo from the left — the two sides meeting down the page, the kitchen–bar handshake. The About photo enters from the right like Bar's (same layout). Card grids (Highlights, Events) all enter from the left, in reading order.
- **Contacts stays nearly still** — `rise` only, no masks: it is the wind-down.
- **Phones:** Highlights and Events are swipe rails there; dealing cards one by one would fire mid-swipe, so the rail rises as one piece instead. Everything else is the same plan.
- **Nothing stays parked when the page changes under a still viewport (20 Sep 2026).** The engine looks again on scroll, resize and load — so two cases left a headline hidden for good, found as a phone menu rail with no current word. *A parked element that loses its box* (a resize across a breakpoint turns its wrapper into `display: contents` / `none` — DevTools' device toolbar, a tablet rotating) is handed back unplayed: it is simply there. *A change with no scroll* (the phone menu swapping its section — every section lands on the same spot) asks for another look with a `reveal:check` event; anything that swaps content in place should send the same.
- **A pill inside a sliding photo is sequenced off its parent** (550 ms after it starts) and never tracked on its own — while the photo is parked its clip hides the pill, and on the first phone pass a fast scroll carried it off before it could pop.
- **Triggering is by layout position, not IntersectionObserver (changed 18 Sep 2026, during the About pass).** A parked element is translated a full width away and clipped to nothing, often inside an `overflow: hidden` parent; an observer sees it as there or not there depending on a pixel of clip margin — the About pairing photo stopped playing the moment its parked clip was tightened. The engine now walks `offsetTop` up the `offsetParent` chain (layout boxes ignore transforms and clips), so the line means the same for every effect: one passive scroll listener, one rAF, ~80 elements. Replay and the lazy-photo wake-up run in the same check.
- **Effects ride `translate` / `scale`, not `transform`** (same date), so they compose with a transform the element already owns — the 1° quote cards, the ±4° perk stamps. With `transform` an entrance would flatten the tilt and snap it back at the end. **The corollary (19 Sep 2026): an element the engine reveals must not be *positioned* with `translate` or `scale`** — `.is-armed.is-in` sets both to `none` for the length of the entrance. The picker's Shake It! button was centred with `translate: -50% -50%` and popped at the right photo's edge until it moved to `calc(50% − 24px)` offsets. **Parked clips are 0 on the leading side** (−24px only once landed): anything hanging past that edge — the ticket's scallops, a tilted corner, a shadow — otherwise shows at the far side of the mask before the element has moved.
- **Two costs handled in the engine:** a parked element clips its own lazy photos, which then never start loading — the check switches them to eager two and a half screens early (lazy-loading is otherwise kept). And a parked `mask-right` element reaches past the viewport, so its section gets `overflow-x: clip` (`.has-reveal`).
- **Checked** at 1440 × 900, 820 × 1180 and 402 × 874 by stepping through the whole page: every armed element released, no image unloaded, `scrollWidth === innerWidth`, no console errors.

### Scroll-in entrances — About (built 18 Sep 2026, author to review)

Same engine, a second plan (`ABOUT` in `reveal.js`), scoped to `.page-about` so the shared Location band and dish picker don't move on the menu page. The Story timeline and ledger keep their own entrance and How it feels its own clock — the plan only touches what those scripts don't move.

- **Headers everywhere:** eyebrow, description, CTAs `rise`; Molot headlines `mask-up`. Where a two-part title flows as one line (phones, tablets) its parts are inline and can't be transformed, so the line's block plays instead.
- **Concept — the handshake, literally.** The plate enters from the left and the glass from the right, the Shake It! button pops on the seam as they meet (+350 ms — the ×'s beat; the button replaced it on 18 Sep 2026), and **the ticket prints**: `mask-down`, out from under the photos (+500 ms). That is The bartender's ticket → *it prints, never fades*, applied once on entry. **Once only — the ticket is not in the replay set**: a slip that re-prints on every pass stops being a reply and becomes decoration. A *pick* re-prints it — see The bartender's ticket → Motion.
- **How it feels:** the cloud and the quote rail `rise` as whole pieces. Per-word reveals would fight the cloud's own placement and orbit.
- **Perks:** the six stamps `pop` in order, each landing at its own tilt; the detail line rises.
- **Story:** the founder photo enters from the right, his quote card rises; headers and body rise. Timeline and ledger untouched.
- **Guests:** the eight photos deal from the left by row, each caption pill popping as its photo lands. **Team:** the photo wall slides in from the right as one strip; the eight cards deal by row. The "Ask me about…" chips are controls and don't move on their own.
- **Careers** (cards `rise`, no masks), **Location** (copy rises, the title block rises behind its mask, the map enters from the right), **Closing invitation** (headline behind its mask, body and buttons rise — the wind-down).
- **Replay** applies as on Home: photos, cards, the wall and the map re-park a screen below the fold; copy, stamps and the ticket play once.
- **Entrance photo drift — the page's one photo parallax.** "The way in" is a 4:1 (21:9 below desktop) cover crop of a much taller photo, so the drift moves the *crop window* (`object-position` 70% → 30%) instead of scaling the picture — a view through a doorway, with none of the home drift's cost (no tighter crop, no upscaling). About only; the menu page's band keeps its authored crop. **Connector lag** now runs on the live-text reflections too, on `translate` so it composes with their `scaleY(-1)`.
- **Checked** at 1440 × 900, 820 × 1180 and 402 × 874 by stepping through the page, plus down → up → down at 1440: everything armed was released, no image unloaded, no horizontal scroll, containers ×2 / copy and ticket ×1, nothing parked in or above the viewport on the way up; Home re-run after the engine change, same results. Not yet on a real device.

**Replay from below — a trial (built 18 Sep 2026, author to keep or drop after a week of real use).** The "once" above now has one exception. Containers — `mask-left` / `mask-right`: the cards and the three preview photos, with the pill a photo carries — **re-park once they are a full screen below the fold**, unseen, and deal again when the guest comes back down. Nothing ever re-parks in view or above the viewport, so a guest scrolling *up* — looking for a price, the phone number, Reserve — always finds a finished page; that is the line between this and scroll-out exits, which stay rejected. Copy (`rise`, `mask-up`) still plays once: re-reading text should never wait on motion. Known edge: the Highlights cards sit within one screen of the top of the page, so on a desktop they can never be a full screen below the fold and do not replay. Checked down → up → down at 1440 and 402: containers played twice, copy once, nothing parked in or above the viewport on the way up, nothing left stuck. One constant, `REPLAY` in `reveal.js` (`null` = once only).

**Connector parallax.** The reflection at a section's head lags the scroll by half its height (≈ 30px desktop, ≈ 10px phone) and closes on the word above as the seam reaches mid-viewport; from there it is the documented pair. CSS scroll-driven (`animation-timeline: view()`), no JS; unsupported browsers show the pair closed, which is the finished state. Scoped to section heads — the menu page's foot words share the `--reflection` class for its opacity and are excluded (fixed the same day after they sank behind the next section; debt: the class means two things). It stays inside *Section connectors*: non-interactive, never fills, no hover. **Heads only — bug found and fixed 18 Sep 2026:** the rule targeted `.section-link-word--reflection`, but on the menu page that class is also worn by the upright word at a section's *foot* (the hero's "food menu", Highlights' word, every section's bottom word, the pairing station's) — it borrows the class for its 50% opacity. Parked 50% down, those words sank behind the next section's ground and read as cut in half (the author saw it on the menu hero and took it for a type-style fault). The parallax now excludes the four foot hooks. *Debt: `--reflection` means two things — "mirrored twin at a head" and "drawn at 50%". A `--faded` modifier for the second would let the parallax target heads positively instead of by exclusion; ~25 template calls, not done.*

**Count-up — "12 years" (built 18 Sep 2026).** The About preview's first stat counts 0 → 12 once, as its row rises, the Story ledger's gesture — the home page quoting the page it links to. **Not the ledger's timing** (retimed the same day, author: barely noticeable): 1.4 s of ease-out cubic suits tens of thousands, but twelve steps on it were over before the row had faded in. It now waits 600 ms, until the row is fully visible showing 0, then takes 2.4 s on ease-out quad — every step readable, the last ones slowest. `src/js/count-up.js`; markup opts in with `data-count-up` on the label (the one template attribute this pass added). The server prints "12 years", so no JS, reduced motion and already-scrolled-past all show the final number; the digit span holds its final width so "years" and the next chip don't shuffle. **Runs once and is not live**, so it stays clear of the no-clock rule and of the reasons study D was parked (a *ticking* number passing an estimate off as data). "5.0 on Yandex" deliberately does not count — a rating sweeping through 3.2 on its way up says the wrong thing. RU: the pattern is *digits then words* ("12 лет"); it reads the number from the text, so no change needed.

**Photo drift (built 18 Sep 2026 — a trial, author to keep or drop).** The three preview photos (Bar, Kitchen, About) sit 12% large in their 3:2 frames and drift ±4% against the scroll, so the photo reads as behind its frame. CSS scroll-driven, in `reveal.css`; the frame is `overflow: hidden`, which makes it a scroll container, so it names the view timeline and the image rides it. Measured at 1440 and 402: the image covers its frame at every scroll position. **Costs, stated:** every preview photo loses 12% to the crop (the bagel is visibly tighter) and is upscaled 12%; and the page now has **two** parallax ideas — I argued for one. If one goes, this is the one: the connectors' parallax explains a pair, this one only adds depth. Browsers without scroll-driven animations, and reduced motion, show the photo unscaled and still.

### Two divergences found while wiring this (not changed)

- **Menu — food state does not theme by the hour in the build.** The table in *What themes and what doesn't* says it does; the engine only auto-themes where there are tiles (home), so `/menu/` from the nav is always day. The head script mirrors the build, not the table. Decide which is right.
- ✓ **Closed 20 Sep 2026 — one bar clock** (see *Open (desktop hero)* → Closed state → Whose clock). Was: **The hero's "now" is the visitor's clock; the Visit hero's is Moscow time** (`visit-hero.js`). A guest abroad — the portfolio audience — sees lunch while the bar is on cocktails. Probably wants the venue's time in both; one line in `inc/daypart-head.php` if so.

## Grid

Full-width/full-bleed sections use a 1120px content width (centered on the page, 80px side margin at the 1280 design width), split into a 12-column grid with a 24px gutter (column ≈ 71.33px — re-checked Sep 2026: 12 × 71.33 + 11 × 24 = 1120 exactly).

**The side gutter ramps; it does not step (revised Sep 2026).** One declaration carries every width, and it replaces the old 16 → 24 → 80 steps:

```css
padding-inline: clamp(16px, 12.5vw - 80px, 80px);
```

`12.5vw − 80px` is one-eighth of the viewport less 80, which passes through exactly **16px at 768** and exactly **80px at 1280**, so both authored endpoints survive and the phone gutter below 768 is untouched. In the ramping band the content column is **0.75 × viewport + 160** (verified against the browser at nine widths).

It replaces a hard 24 → 80 step at 992 that made the content column *narrow as the window got wider* — 943 at 991, 832 at 992 — dragging everything sized off it backwards: hero tile 186 → 162, cards 298 → 261, connector SVG 943 → 832, and the section headline from two lines to three. A second, smaller step at 767/768 (735 → 720) went with it. `vw` rather than `%` because one `.container` on the menu page sits inside `.menu-visit-cta`, where a percentage would resolve against the wrong box; the cost is that `vw` counts the scrollbar (~1.6px on the gutter).

### Icon beside text (18 Sep 2026)
Rule and the measured numbers: `design.md` §5.2 → Icon beside text. In the build: `.contact-item__icon` (the `contactItem` component — 4px, already as drawn), the footer's `.contact-item i` (**was 0 — the icons sat 3.6px above the capitals; now 4px**, found by the author on the address row), `.visit-hero__event-icon` (4px), and `.dish-option-bullet` (6 → **8px**, re-centred after the option text went from 14 to 16 the same day). Note the footer and the component share the class name `contact-item` but are two different builds (`footer.css` with the Phosphor webfont `<i>`, `contacts.css` with inlined SVG) — the footer's icons carry no state, so the webfont is within `design.md` §5.2 → Delivery, but the shared name is a trap: a change to one does not reach the other.

### Spacing — the 4px rule (documented Aug 2026, from the Figma file)

**Every spacing value is a multiple of 4.** That is the whole rule; there is no separate named-token vocabulary to learn, and nothing needs a name like `space-md`.

- **Spacing scale (Figma `Spacing` collection):** 4 · 8 · 12 · 16 · 24 · 36 · 44 · 52 · 64 · 80
- **Icon sizes (Figma `Sizes` → `Icons`):** xs 8 · s 12 · m 16 · l 24 · xl 36 · xxl 48

Both are subsets of the 4px grid, not separate systems. Use a scale value where one fits; any other multiple of 4 is legal when it doesn't. The now-marker's 8px dot (The day/night concept) is on this grid, as is the 24px column gutter above.

**Carve-out — the page gutter interpolates (Sep 2026).** The ramped side gutter above computes to off-grid values between its endpoints (20 · 33 · 44 · 60 · 70). The *authored* values are still 16 and 80, both on the grid; what the clamp produces between them is interpolation, exactly like a fluid type scale. The rule governs gaps that things stack and align against, and nothing aligns against a page margin — the 24px column gutter, every internal gap and every spacing token are unaffected. A `round(down, …, 4px)` variant was built and rejected: quantising keeps every computed gutter on the grid, but it turns the content width into a sawtooth that climbs ~37px then drops 8px at each step, trading one 111px cliff for fourteen 8px ones.

*Note for the Figma file, not for the build:* spacing and icon sizes are each modelled as **one variable with many modes** rather than many variables. It works, but modes are a theming axis — a frame sits in one spacing mode at a time, so nested elements needing different gaps need nested overrides. No effect on the CSS, which just gets the scale above.

**Body copy is capped by column count, not by character or word count.** A live word-count-driven rule renders inconsistently depending on the actual copy dropped in; a fixed column span stays predictable regardless of content.

**Desktop: body copy caps by column count** inside full-width sections, even when the section itself is full-bleed — never the full 12. Narrower breakpoints get their own column rule; don't assume the desktop span holds below desktop (Tablet, below, now defines it).

> **Open — this entry and the build disagree, and always have (found Sep 2026).** The doc says **6 of 12 columns (548px)**. `.section-description` in `components.css` says **643px**, which is exactly **7 of 12** (7 × 71.33 + 6 × 24 = 643.33). Both are grid-legal and nobody has been reading the doc's number, so this is a naming fix, not a layout bug — pick one and make the other match; `components.css` is the single place it lives. Its sibling `.section-title-block` = **739px** checks out as exactly **8 of 12** (8 × 71.33 + 7 × 24 = 738.67) and is correct as authored.

**Mobile (defined Sep 2026):** the Figma frame is 402 wide with **16px gutters → 370 content**; body copy runs the full 370. The hero bento is **3 equal columns with a 12px gap** — **20 between the two rows since 20 Sep 2026** (12 + the 8 the lit tile's sheets need; Mobile — home hero → Anatomy) — (wide slot = 2 columns + gap ≈ 242, narrow ≈ 115), everything else is single-column. In code: the nav collapses at **≤ 991px**, phone layouts and the 16px gutter apply at **≤ 767px**; the 4px rule holds throughout (see the carve-out above for the page gutter alone). The drawer uses 24px gutters (its own frame).

The leftover columns get a job, not left blank — which one depends on how much copy there is:
- **Short copy** (2–4 sentence teasers, e.g. News cards): flush-left the text to the same grid start as the headline above it, and pair the remaining columns with a secondary element — a stat, a pull-quote, or social icons. Never centered — centering a narrow column in a wide section reads as an accident, not a choice.
- **Long-form copy** (blog posts, About page body, full menu item descriptions): split into two columns of running text side by side instead of one narrow column plus empty space — the standard editorial fix for reconciling full width with a readable measure.

### Tablet (768–991, built Sep 2026 — with the section header running to 1151)

No separate tablet composition was drawn and none is needed: **tablet is phone chrome over desktop content.** The nav collapses to the drawer at ≤ 991 while the body keeps the desktop grid, and the ramped gutter above removes the need for a tablet container of its own.

**The band has two bounds, deliberately.** Chrome flips at **991** (the nav, the drawer, the hidden Reserve tab). The section header flips at **1152**, because that is where its two halves actually fit — see below. One number was chosen for the chrome and the other derived from the content, and they are not meant to match; a rule that reaches past 991 is not a bug unless it has no arithmetic behind it. What the band does need is the handful of places where a desktop rule stops being true, or where a phone rule was labelled 991 by accident. Nothing here was drawn in Figma first; it was measured in the browser and the numbers below are from that pass.

**Percentage widths authored against the 1120 column are the recurring fault.** Each holds at the design width and squeezes everywhere below it. Three were found; expect more on the inner pages.

- `.about-preview-text` was `flex: 0 0 55%` — 396–519px in the band, which broke the Molot headline to three lines. **`width: 100%` cannot override it:** on the main axis flex-basis wins. Stacking the parent then re-caps the block at the description's 643, because `align-items: flex-start` makes a column item shrink-to-fit. Two independent caps, and which one binds depends on whether the parent is a row or a column — check both before concluding a width is unsettable.
- `.section-title-block` was `width: 66%` — the fluid equivalent of 739 only at a 1120 column, and 549 at an 832 one. Now `min(100%, 739px)`: the authored Figma number, correct at every width.
- `.section-ctas .btn` is a fixed **261px** inside a slot only 221px wide at 768. Right-aligned, so the overflow pushed left *into the paragraph* — 8px into Highlights, 16px into Contacts, 2px into Contacts still at 834. A real collision, not tightness.

**What stacks.** Both menu previews and the About preview take the phone's single column, photo first, from ≤ 991. The menu page goes further and takes the phone model whole — one section at a time — see Tablet — Menu page for why it is the exception to "phone chrome over desktop content". The `≤767` blocks were **widened rather than copied**, with the phone-only extras (48px section rhythm, 12px stat icon, full-width CTA, 16px caption inset) left behind in a `≤767` block — two near-identical media blocks would have drifted. The argument for stacking rather than keeping the split: at 768–991 the two 50% halves leave the dish list **350–434px, narrower than the 370 a phone gives the same rows.** The About preview follows the phone's ordering exactly (eyebrow → headlines → photo → description → stats → CTA), not photo-last.

**The section header stacks to 1151, not 991 — and the bound is derived, not chosen.** Side by side the header needs 739 (title) + 24 (gap) + 261 (button) = **1024 of content**, and the content column is 0.75 × viewport + 160, so 1024 arrives at exactly **1152**. Below that the two halves do not both fit, and the choice is a squeezed three-line headline or the stack; the headline is the section's identity, so it wins. The CTA slot runs as a **row of equal buttons** there rather than the phone's column of full-width ones — two stacked full-width buttons across 736–1023px read as a form, not as a pair of doors (Interaction rule). Re-derive the bound if the gutter ramp, the 739 or the 261 ever move, and **check it again when Polylang lands: RU labels are longer than EN.**

**Contacts runs full width in the band** (author's call, Sep 2026). The `≤991` block that stacks `.contacts-split` stays as it is — map above form, form across the column. Recorded cost: the form field measure, inputs at 620–843px. Separable from the layout if it ever wants capping. Two map fixes went with it:

- **The address bar stays a row.** It had been stacked here only because the `≤991` block was written for phones — and below 767 the bar is `display: none` anyway (phones use `.contacts-map__chips--phone`), so the stacking rule had no phone to run on. In the band the bar is 734–901 wide against a row needing ~466, so it fits with room and keeps its desktop height, 56 rather than 91.
- **The map is taller, and fluid.** `min-height: clamp(400px, min(60vw, 55vh), 600px)` replaces an inherited phone 300. 600 reads well on a desktop browser narrowed to this width but eats a real tablet screen, so width drives the height — which also keeps the map near 3:2 against the content column — and the `vh` term guards short landscape viewports. Measured: 461 at 768×1024, 500 at 834×1112, 547 at 912×1368, 595 at 991, and 400 at 960×600 where the guard bites. `vh` not `dvh`, so the map does not resize while a mobile toolbar hides.

**Open — the stacked photo.** Full-bleed 3:2 is 786 × 524 at 834 and **903 × 602 at 991**, larger than anything on the desktop page (1280 gives 542 × 375), and it makes the band 23–32% taller than desktop (8,716–9,363px against 7,095). The same rule already runs at 700–767, so it is not new — just far more visible at 903. Two ways out and the docs pull against each other: cap the stacked photo at ~560 flush left and give the leftover columns a job (Short copy, above), or let it take the **4:1** band ratio, which is exactly the divider job 4:1 was added for — except Image ratios says 4:1 and 21:9 are *never used for content cards*. Undecided; resolve before launch.

**The About page has its own entry.** Tablet — About page records that band separately, because About is a fixed composition with eleven sections of its own and the faults there were content-loss, not awkwardness. The general lesson from it is worth keeping here: **a fixed pixel column inside a fluid one is this project's recurring tablet bug**, and it does not always look like one — a `flex-basis` written as a width on desktop silently becomes a height the moment the container flips to a column.

**The home hero in the band — the phone bento, on portrait tablets (author, Sep 2026).** The row was measured first: four **137–174px** squares across a 720–900 column read as thumbnails, the active tile's size advantage was the desktop's weak 15–25%, and the hardcoded 76px headline wrapped to two lines below ~870 (*the row's headline takes `--text-display` since 18 Sep 2026 — 64 in the band, one line at 820 × 700; Typography on the web → Tablet mode*), so 768 and 820 showed a two-line Display headline over a strip of small photos — the desktop stack narrowed, the same fault as the desktop's empty right third. The phone bento at the same widths: the active photo **473 × 320 at 768, 499 × 335 at 820, 584 × 392 at 991**, narrow tiles 207–256, and the hero still one viewport on every portrait tablet (948 at 768 × 1024, 1104 at 820 × 1180). In practice the 768–991 band *is* portrait tablets — 768 / 810 / 820 / 834, all ≥ 1024 tall; every landscape tablet is 1024+ and takes the desktop row. **Guarded by height, not width:** the bento query is `(min-width: 768px) and (max-width: 991px) and (min-height: 1000px)`, so a short viewport in the band — a landscape phone, a narrowed desktop window — keeps the row; the bento hero is ~1100px tall there and its headline sat 926px down at 991 × 744. **What differs from the phone, in its own block:** the eyebrow and the second CTA return (the phone cut them for space; the pair splits the row), the headline is **64px** — the Typography mobile Display size, not the phone's raw 32; one line for the current copy from 768 — the subhead 20, the grid gap 24 (the grid gutter; the span formula follows), the primary button at its natural width. The language nudge stays hidden as on phones. Colour is Chili in both modes, as on desktop (author, 17 Sep 2026 — the divergence closed the day after it reached three widths). **Costs recorded:** at 768 × 1024 and 991 × 1200 the content is taller than the section's minimum, so the foot word starts 54–67px below the fold (the buttons are above it at every width); the grid-to-headline 32 and the 24px top padding are the slack if the word should sit on the fold. Tap verified at 820: the tile moves into the wide slot on the spring, the theme flips. *Deliberately wrong version considered:* two-sided at tablet — copy left, a 2 × 2 bento right, the desktop's own leading alternative — rejected here because a ~340px copy column runs the 64px headline to three lines and stacks the button pair; worth prototyping at 1120+, where the desktop already wants it. **Single-button CTAs in the band sit left, at natural width (author, 17 September 2026).** The kitchen preview's button had kept its desktop right-alignment (`.menu-preview-cta--right`, the mirrored row's) under a now left-aligned list, 500px from its own text, while the bar preview's and the About preview's sat on the grid start; it is left now, so the three stacked previews agree. Natural width, not full: one full-width button across 736–903 reads as a form field, the same reason the section header's pair stays a row of two (above). Phones keep their full-width CTA. *Wrong version considered:* right-aligning every single CTA as a "read on" exit, the way the Visit ticket's "See what's new →" sits at its right edge — a text-link habit that leaves a button belonging to nothing on a 720–900 column; rejected. **Also noted:** the footer's four columns fit exactly at 768 with the address ending on the container edge, no slack for RU; no horizontal overflow, no clipped nowrap text, section header, events three-up (224–285) and contacts as documented.

### Responsive faults — the five that keep recurring (Sep 2026)

Written after the home, About and Visit tablet passes, because the same handful of faults produced almost every bug in them. Check these before reading a layout as a design problem.

**1. A fixed pixel column inside a fluid one.** The single most common fault — **seven** were found and fixed across the three tablet passes: the team card (260), the About founder column (448), the shared map (600), the About quotes column (540), Visit's map column (736), its hours column (548) and its contacts card (524), plus the home page's 261px CTA button inside a 221px slot. Each holds at the design width and either clips content away or starves the column beside it below that.

*A grep for `width: NNNpx` / `flex: 0 0 NNNpx` finds more that are currently benign — the 356px team wall card (inside the drift rail, which is meant to be wider than the viewport) and the 640px careers empty state (not rendered). Re-check either if it ever lands in normal flow.*

> **The question that resolves it: which column has a hard content requirement?** Pin that one and let the other flex — Visit's badge names must not ellipsise while a map is legible at any size, so the badges are pinned and the map gives. Where **both** can give, give each a `flex-basis` and make the bases sum to the design column, so the design is exact at full width and the pair scales together below it (Visit's hero: `1 1 548px` + `1 1 524px` against a 1072 gap-adjusted column). Where **neither** can, the layout has a derived breakpoint — see 5.

**2. A flex property means something different once the axis flips.** Eight instances — the eighth found a day after this list was written: Visit's badge list kept `flex: 0 0 360px` into the stacked container and became a 360px-tall box, which Chrome and Safari then rendered differently (stretched rows against a gap). `align-items` is vertical alignment in a row and horizontal in a column, so `center` and `flex-end` written for a desktop row silently centre or right-shove every child when the container stacks — Visit's hero message pushed its title block to x=302 against a 24px gutter, and its location header centred the Good-to-know slip with 217px either side. `flex-basis` is worse: it governs width in a row and **height** in a column, so About's quotes column — `flex: 0 0 540px`, a width on desktop — became a 540px-tall box around 200px of content. Nothing about either declaration looks wrong when you read it.

**3. `height: 100%` needs a *definite* parent height.** An iframe with `height: 100%` on an auto-height parent falls back to the **HTML default of 150px** — the Location map painted a 150px strip inside a 500px well. A flex row's `align-items: stretch` makes the height definite, which is why desktop escapes it; a column does not, and `min-height` never does. Use an explicit `height` on the stacked parent.

**4. A transform's overhang scales with the element, not the breakpoint.** A rotation's vertical overhang is a function of **width**: the 1° quote card overhangs ~3px on a 364px phone card and ~8px on an 897px one, against a fixed 4px viewport padding, and the tilted corners clipped. Measure with `getBoundingClientRect()`, which returns the rotated box, rather than padding the container — padding needs a different value at every width.

**5. Derive breakpoints, do not pick them.** Every bound that has survived was arithmetic: the home section header at **1152** (739 + 24 + 261 = 1024 of content, reached at 1152), Visit's hero split at **1120** (where the card stops covering the band), the team grid's fluid card (4 × 260 + 3 × 24 = 1112 never fits a tablet column). Write the arithmetic into the comment so it can be re-derived when the copy, the gap or the component widths move — and **check it again when Polylang lands**, because RU strings are longer than EN.

**6. A boundary between two elements is not a boundary you own.** Two flush surfaces meeting at a fractional y — every section is text-height, so most do — are rasterised separately, and at any non-integer scale (DevTools "fit to window", Windows at 125–150%, a scaled screenshot) a hairline of whatever is *behind* them can show. Overlapping by a pixel only helps when both surfaces are the same colour and the parent behind them is too; the Visit footer's dark hairline was the two Lime footer blocks over the page's Peppercorn body. Two rules make a seam impossible: paint the parent the shared colour (`.site-footer` is Lime now), and where two *different* colours meet, give the boundary to one element as a border plus background painted in one pass (the hours ticket carries a 1px Peppercorn top border; the scallop strip's centre row sits on it). `top: -1px` on a relatively positioned strip is the wrong tool — it moves the paint and leaves the flow box, opening a 1px slot below. Not reproducible at an integer device scale (checked at 2× in headless Chrome), so verify hairlines at 100% before treating them as layout.

**And a measurement note.** Proximity on one axis is not collision. Comparing `band.right` to `card.left` said Visit's contacts card overlapped the state band from 992 to 1175; testing whether the two rectangles actually intersect said 992 to 1119, because above that they are vertically separated. The wrong test produced a plausible, wrong breakpoint.

## Layout notes

- The giant Molot wordmark can sit on the seam between sections: outlined on the dark side, filled crossing into the light side. Replaces the old mirrored-reflection idea.
- Section striping is a deliberate rhythm, but it's not one rule across both states — see Section striping (day / night) below.
- A persistent "Reserve" tab stays on the right edge in all states. On the menu page, past the hero, a matching menu-nav tab holds the left edge (Menu page → Sticky jump-nav).

### Section connectors ("link words")

Documented Aug 2026 — the device existed on the home and menu pages before it had an entry here. Giant outlined Molot set across the full-bleed photo band between sections ("yummy morning", "soul therapy", "the best in the city"). One component, used on both pages.

- **Must be SVG format**: These link words must ALWAYS be exported and placed as SVGs, not rendered as HTML text.
- **Request missing words**: If a particular section requires a link word and you don't have the SVG asset for it, you MUST stop and ask the user to provide it.
- **Width matching**: The width of the SVG link word MUST exactly match the width of the content container (in Figma, this is 80px margins from the viewport). SVGs should be given `width: 100%` inside the standard container. *Mobile (Sep 2026): the container is 370 (402 − 2×16) and the desktop SVG scales to it. By night the scaled SVGs run at full opacity on phones (desktop: 30%) — at a third of their stroke weight, any dimming reads as dark grey on Peppercorn (author, Sep 2026); day keeps 50% at every width. The Figma mobile component (`SectionLinkMobile`) draws live Molot text with a gradient fade instead of the SVG — not adopted; the SVG rule stands until the author says otherwise.*
- **Height reserved before load (Sep 2026).** The SVG component (`section-link-word.php`) reads each SVG's viewBox in PHP (`inc/svg-dimensions.php`) and writes it as `width`/`height` on the `<img>`, so the band has its aspect ratio before the lazy file arrives. Without it every connector is 0px until fetched and the menu page's anchor scrolls (hero nav, jump-nav) land short by the height of every connector above the target. Keep the attributes when touching the markup, and keep `height: auto` in the CSS. *SVG connectors only (home, menu): the live-text prototype on About and Visit reserves nothing until fonts load and `section-link.js` fits the word — harmless while those pages have no long anchor scrolls, but it must be solved before live text reaches the menu page.*
- **`preserveAspectRatio="none"` on every connector SVG (21 Sep 2026).** Found on the test site, iPhone and iPad, home page by day: some reflections drew narrower or wider than their word. Layout was exact (both boxes 370.0 wide at 402, heights to the viewBox ratio) and the files fill their viewBox edge to edge — the fault is in the paint. A connector is ~14:1 and an SVG fits its box with *meet*, so if the engine paints into a rect even a fraction of a pixel short, the whole word shrinks and re-centres, fourteen times as much in width: **a box 0.86px short draws a 370px word at 357, from x=22 — the iPhone screenshot's numbers exactly** (reproduced in Chrome by forcing the height). WebKit does this on its own; Chrome does not at any width or pixel ratio tried. It showed by day only because the day word and its reflection are different sizes (the 2px Olive stroke makes the word's viewBox 2 wider and 1–2 taller than the Lime reflection's), so their errors differ; by night the pair share one viewBox and err together. With `none` the word always spans the box's full width and a short rect costs under a pixel of height instead. All 93 files under `assets/sectionLinks/` carry it. **A Figma export never writes this attribute — re-add it to the root `<svg>` after every re-export.** *Not yet seen on a device: WebKit cannot be driven from this Mac (no Playwright, Safari's remote automation is off). The SVG URLs carry no version string, so a phone that has the old files needs a private tab or a cleared cache to judge it.*
- **Parallax (18 Sep 2026).** The reflection at a section's *head* lags the scroll and closes on the word above (Motion language → Scroll-in entrances → Connector parallax). **Heads only:** on the menu page the upright foot words wear `--reflection` for its opacity and must not move — the rule in `reveal.css` excludes them (`report.md`, 18 Sep). The hero's foot word is `loading="eager"`; every other connector stays lazy.
- **Non-interactive — and that is the specification, not an omission.** A connector is not a link, not a section heading, and not the photo's caption. It must never acquire a hover fill, a trailing arrow, or a block: those are the three tappable grammars (Interaction rule), and borrowing one would make a decorative word claim to be a door or a chip.
- **Why outlined Molot here doesn't dilute outline → fill.** Elsewhere outline means *not current* and filling means *active*. Connectors never fill, so they'd be the one place outline is merely texture. The carve-out that resolves it is **scale register**: outline → fill governs Molot at *reading and interaction* sizes — word lists, slider labels, nav items, chapter rails. Giant display Molot that bleeds off the layout is a separate register where outline is a treatment, not a state. The room wordmark and the seam wordmark (above) already live in that register; connectors join it. Keep the two registers visibly far apart in size — if a connector ever shrinks to near-H1, it re-enters the state vocabulary and the carve-out stops protecting it.
- **Two jobs, one component (known, deliberate).** On the home page a connector is a forward invitation to the section below. On the menu page it supports the section it travels with. The author's call is that the menu-page reading is the better fit for a dense list page.
- **Open — the direction problem.** A word sitting *between* two sections has no inherent referent; position can't tell the guest whether it looks forward or back, so today it's resolved only by reading the words. Fix by pinning the referent structurally rather than semantically: either every connector band belongs to the section **below** it (becoming its epigraph, which also unifies with the home page), or every band belongs to the section **above**. Pick one, move the bands, and rewrite the copy that no longer fits. Until then, expect the odd misread on first visit. *Visit (Sep 2026) adopts the first reading outright: its two connectors — YOUR ROUTE TO PEPPER at the hero's foot, DROP A LITTLE NOTE at Location's foot — each name the section below, and the page is recorded as "a band belongs to the section it opens". If that reading is later chosen site-wide, Visit is already compliant; if the other one is, Visit's two words have to move.*
- **Voice — review them as a set, not one at a time.** Section names are plain and each list gets exactly one voiced name; connectors are a separate register and aren't bound by that count. But eight-plus winks down a single page is a lot of personality per scroll, and the tone only reads as a set. Cut the weakest before launch rather than defending each on its own.
- **Copy lives in Figma only.** The home- and menu-page connector wording isn't in any project file yet — pull it in so it can be voiced in RU as register twins rather than translated late.

## Section striping (day / night)

The dark/light/accent section rhythm runs on different logic in each state (decided in the home-page IA work). Value alternation — not a literal colour swap — is what carries it, so a photographic section and a flat-colour section can share a value without reading as a repeated beat.

**Day — clean alternation.** Sections alternate light/dark down the page (Paper/Parchment ↔ Peppercorn), with a saturated band (Lemon) used sparingly as the one "colour-block moment" (§2.4 allows saturated fills for banners only). One sanctioned adjacency: the Hero and the section directly below it (Highlights) may both sit light — the Hero is photo-with-overlay, Highlights is flat cream, different enough in texture that it doesn't read as monotonous.

**Night — immersive dark.** Night does *not* checkerboard; it commits to darkness and lets Lime/Chili accents glow out of shared dark — "being in the bar after dark" rather than a daytime-poster rhythm. Break up long dark runs with **Soft Peppercorn** `#201E22` (`design.md` §2.2 — a ~4-point luminance lift over Peppercorn: texture without contrast), **not** Dark Olive. Let one bright section (e.g. Lunch) be the single point of light in the middle. Optional night softener: light sections that stay light at night can step Paper→Parchment to shrink the value jump at each seam — not required.

**Contact section — dark in both modes.** The closing zone reads as the wind-down regardless of daypart. At night this is simply consistent with everything around it; in day it's a deliberate exception (it accepts one two-dark run, News→Contact) chosen so Contact always feels like the wind-down zone. **Scope revision (Jul 2026):** this rule covers the *home page's closing section* only. **Corrected Aug 2026:** the Visit page does not theme by time either — the Jul revision assumed it did. Visit and About are **fixed compositions** (The day/night concept → What themes and what doesn't); Visit runs dark · light · dark into the Lime footer, in every state. Its sections don't swap clothes, so the striping rhythm is authored once rather than twice.

**Footer — saturated Lime in both modes (revised Aug 2026).** The footer used to fall under the rule above; it no longer does. It is the one place on the site that **stops theming altogether** — a fixed landmark at the end of every page, in both day and night. That's a more useful rule than a wind-down that changes clothes, and it gives the closing zone a single identity across the whole site.

- **The scalloped edge belongs to the section above (Aug 2026).** The footer's Lime ground never changes, but its scalloped top edge takes the colour of whatever section it meets — Paper, Parchment, Peppercorn or Soft Peppercorn. The seam is the last beat of the section it leaves rather than the first beat of the footer, which is what lets one fixed Lime band sit under every possible ground without a hard collision. The scallop is the bottle-cap motif (`design.md` §5.1); it is **not** a bartender's ticket and must not acquire slip anatomy — see the bullet below.
- **Consequences.** The footer is now the brightest object on any night page, so it can't also hold a bartender's ticket — the slip depends on being the brightest thing in view (`design.md` §5.1, revised to match). One closing device per page, and Lime is it. If the Peppercorn → Lime seam reads harsh at night, step the section directly above the footer to Soft Peppercorn so there's a beat before the green.
- **Text on Lime is constrained.** Peppercorn (12:1) and Ash (5.1:1) only. **Olive fails at 2.86:1** and Chili at 2.65:1 — see `design.md` §2.5, where the old "passes on Lemon/Lime" claim was corrected. Olive is the easy mistake here, since muted-green-on-green looks right and measures wrong.
- **GO TO mirrors the top nav (decided Aug 2026): Home · Menu · About · Visit.** Events is removed — it stays out of every nav, header and footer alike, until its page exists (Top nav; Launch phasing). Mockups carried an Events link in the footer; that is the one to correct, not the rule.
- **Mobile: two columns, not four stacked groups.** Stacking turns a ~350px desktop band into ~1,050px — 1.2 full screens of unbroken saturated green, at which point it stops reading as a band and starts reading as a page. Pairing GO TO with HOURS (both short) and running REACH US full width brings it to roughly 610px, which fits one screen and preserves the desktop proportion. Hours labels stack above their values rather than sitting beside them; that's what makes the two-column split fit at 402px.

## Image ratios

The image component ships six fixed ratios — one per content job, not one per breakpoint:

| Ratio | Use |
|---|---|
